UNIVERSITY: University of Trento
COURSE: [0115G] BUSINESS ADMINISTRATION AND LAW
TYPE: Bachelor's Degree
SECTION: General Description
URL: https://unitn.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10126

[0115G] BUSINESS ADMINISTRATION AND LAW Bachelor's Degree
COURSE INFORMATION General information relating to the current academic year. Duration: 3 years. Admission year: 2011. Status: Open. Credits: 180 CFU. Type: Three-year Bachelor's Degree. Category: Economics, Management and Law. Regulatory Framework: 270/04. Degree Class: L-18 - Class of degrees in Economics and Business Management. Inter-university Course: No. Programmed Access (140 places). Location: City Campus - Economics - via Inama, 5 (Sustainable capacity: 140). Department: Department of Economics and Management. Verification of knowledge required for access: No. Program, texts and objectives: Specific educational objectives of the course and description of the educational path. The Bachelor's Degree in Business Administration and Law trains graduates who possess an integrated set of economic, business administration and legal knowledge, as well as practical skills and communication techniques, aimed at the administration of economic organizations. The graduate in Business Administration and Law will be able to operate in accounting, administrative, legal and tax fields, both as an external professional within regulated economic and legal professions, and as an employee within companies and private and public organizations.
The Bachelor's Degree in Business Administration and Law aims to integrate business and economic training with legal knowledge in areas of direct interest to private and public companies and organizations.
The course of study is structured with a common part for all courses of study in the same class, in which students are exposed to theoretical and practical knowledge in the areas of business administration, law (private, public and commercial), economics and basic knowledge in mathematical and statistical fields.
In the second part of the course, students deepen their knowledge in specific legal and business areas. The degree course includes a mandatory internship. The internship can be organized in collaboration with the Professional Orders of the Triveneto region based on a specific agreement.
The course of study includes laboratories, organized in collaboration with professionals, in which theoretical knowledge is translated into practical contexts within simulations of real problems.
The didactic regulations of the course of study may provide for the activation of paths or training curricula.
Knowledge required for access: The student enrolling in the Bachelor's Degree in Business Administration and Law is required to demonstrate possession of a minimum level of knowledge in the following areas:
- mastery of the basic tools of logic and mathematics with particular reference to:
° elementary algebraic calculation: powers, absolute value,
polynomials, first and second degree equations and inequalities;
° fundamental notions of analytical geometry: line,
circle, parabola, ellipse and hyperbola;
- mastery of a foreign language at level B1, among French, English, German and Spanish;
- mastery of personal productivity applications (Open Office, Microsoft Office, etc.), ECDL level, or equivalent (4 basic modules, designed to be integrated with learning during the course of study, up to ECDL Full level, or equivalent).
The knowledge required for access is verified, according to the methods defined by the Didactic Regulations of the course of study, which also identifies any additional training obligations in the event of a negative verification.

UNIVERSITY: University of Trento
COURSE: [0116G] BUSINESS MANAGEMENT
TYPE: Bachelor's Degree
SECTION: General Description
URL: https://unitn.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10128

[0116G] BUSINESS MANAGEMENT Bachelor's Degree
COURSE INFORMATION General information for the current academic year Duration: 3 years. Established: 2011. Status: Open. Credits: 180 CFU. Type: Three-year Bachelor's Degree. Category: Economics, Management and Law. Regulatory Framework: 270/04. Degree Class: L-18 - Class of Degrees in Economics and Business Management. Inter-University Course: No. Limited Access (260 places). Location: City Campus - Economics - via Inama, 5 (Sustainable capacity: 260). Department: Department of Economics and Management. Verification of required knowledge for access: No. Program, texts and objectives: Specific learning objectives of the course and description of the educational path. The Bachelor's Degree in Business Management aims to train professionals capable of understanding, managing and improving the processes through which companies create value, efficiently transforming production factors into goods and services that meet the needs of consumers or users. To develop these skills, Business Management graduates learn the tools for detection and analysis and the decision-making models typical of management disciplines, and are guided in their application, also using appropriate mathematical and statistical methodologies and IT tools. They also know how to grasp the connections between the company and the economic and regulatory context, thanks to the acquisition of the fundamental interpretative tools of economics and law.
In the first year, common to all degree courses in the same class, students explore the typical themes of Economics, Business Economics and Law, as well as acquiring the necessary basic mathematical-analytical and statistical skills.
Subsequently, the Business Management degree course provides specific in-depth studies in the various fields of management knowledge, legal integrations for commercial aspects and the study of foreign languages, with some limited degrees of freedom for students in their choices.
A large amount of space is reserved for laboratory activities, where statistical methodological knowledge and economic-business knowledge interact in defining a learning path linked to the reality of companies and problem solving. In these activities, the acquisition and use of communication skills play an important role.
Internships in companies are mandatory for all students enrolled in the degree course.
Specific training contracts can be activated based on the commitment regime (part-time or full-time) of the students.
Knowledge required for access: The student enrolling in the Business Management degree course is required to demonstrate possession of a minimum level of knowledge in the following areas:
- mastery of the basic tools of logic and mathematics with particular reference to:
° elementary algebraic calculation: powers, absolute value,
polynomials, first and second degree equations and inequalities;
° fundamental notions of analytical geometry: straight line,
circle, parabola, ellipse and hyperbola;
- mastery of a foreign language at level B1, among French, English, German and Spanish;
- mastery of personal productivity applications (Open Office, Microsoft Office, etc.), ECDL level, or equivalent (4 basic modules, designed to be integrated with learning during the course of study, up to ECDL Full level, or equivalent).
The verification of the required knowledge for access is provided, according to the methods defined by the didactic regulations of the degree course, which also identifies the additional training obligations in the event of a negative verification.

UNIVERSITY: University of Trento
COURSE: [0117G] ECONOMICS AND MANAGEMENT
TYPE: Bachelor's Degree
SECTION: General Description
URL: https://unitn.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10129

[0117G] ECONOMICS AND MANAGEMENT Bachelor's Degree
COURSE INFORMATION General information relating to the current academic year. Duration: 3 years. Admission year: 2011. Status: Open. Credits: 180 CFU. Type: Three-year Bachelor's degree. Category: Economics, Management and Law. Regulatory Framework: Reform 270/04. Degree Class: L-33 - Class of degrees in Economics. Interclass: L-18 - Class of degrees in Economics and Business Management. Inter-university Course: No. Programmed Access (235 places). Location: City Campus - Economics - via Inama, 5 (Sustainable capacity: 235). Department: Department of Economics and Management. Verification of knowledge required for access: No. Program, texts and objectives: Specific learning objectives of the course and description of the educational path. The Bachelor's Degree in Economics and Management aims to provide a solid methodological foundation in economics and management sciences, enabling students to correctly and effectively master economic analysis at both the country and company levels, with particular attention to the growing interrelationships between the two areas. Graduates in Economics and Management will be able to directly and independently address tasks and decision-making problems in various fields, using up-to-date analytical, measurement, and forecasting tools. The Bachelor's Degree in Economics and Management also aims to provide training that is open to continuous learning and innovation, allowing graduates to subsequently access the latest developments in the field during their professional career or further education.
The Economics and Management course is a course with an exclusively "methodological" focus and is therefore strongly oriented towards providing solid and in-depth basic knowledge in the reference disciplines and analytical tools. To this end, particular emphasis is placed on the acquisition of mathematical and statistical tools, with particular attention to the inferential aspects of statistics.
The course of study includes an initial part dedicated to the fundamentals of economic and management disciplines, with theoretical and methodological in-depth study. In particular, the basic knowledge of economics and management will be explored, with interrelationships between the two areas, especially with regard to markets, consumer behavior, and competition. In this area of basic training, the typical themes of regulation and the formation of the norms that underpin them will be addressed. In addition, there will also be courses with a general educational value, such as economic history.
In the second part of the course, students will explore some areas of theory typical of the common areas of interest of economics and management, paying attention to the theme of economic decision-making. This theme will be explored both at the analytical level and at the level of behavioral aspects, the study of which is now a fundamental part of the knowledge base for those involved in economic decisions, both at the aggregate level and at the company level. This is in line with the prevailing orientations of the Faculty, which are oriented towards behavioral studies.
Finally, students will have the opportunity to orient their training by choosing from a range of thematic in-depth courses in the various areas of study previously addressed. A key moment is the multidisciplinary laboratory activity, where the theoretical knowledge and analytical tools acquired will be used to acquire the practical ability to use advanced tools for evaluating decisions, in particular using simulation techniques.
The linguistic skills verified upon entry will be reinforced with a "specific" language course for the field of study and teaching activities conducted in English.
The thesis, typically of a theoretical-methodological nature, concludes the course.
Knowledge required for access: Students enrolling in the Bachelor's Degree in Economics and Management are required to demonstrate possession of a minimum level of knowledge in the following areas:
- mastery of the basic tools of logic and mathematics, with particular reference to:
• elementary algebraic calculation: powers, absolute value, polynomials, first and second degree equations and inequalities;
• fundamental notions of analytical geometry: line, circle, parabola, ellipse and hyperbola;
- mastery of a foreign language at level B1, among French, English, German and Spanish;
- mastery of personal productivity applications (Open Office, Microsoft Office, etc.), ECDL level or equivalent (4 basic modules, to be integrated with learning during the course of study, up to ECDL Full or equivalent).
Verification of the knowledge required for access is provided, according to the methods defined by the didactic regulations of the course of study, which also identifies any additional training obligations in the event of a negative verification.

UNIVERSITY: University of Trento
COURSE: [0123H] MANAGEMENT
TYPE: Master's Degree
SECTION: General Description
URL: https://unitn.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10185

[0123H] MANAGEMENT Master's Degree - English
COURSE INFORMATION General information relating to the current academic year Duration: 2 years. Program: 2011. Status: Open. Credits: 120 ECTS. Type: Master's Degree Courses. Category: Economics, Management and Law. Regulatory Framework: Reform 270/04. Degree Class: LM-77 - Master's Degree in Business and Economics. Inter-University Course: No. Programmed Access (100 places). Location: City Campus - Economics - via Inama, 5 (Sustainable capacity: 100). Department: Department of Economics and Management. Verification of knowledge required for access: No. Program, texts and objectives: Specific learning objectives of the course and description of the educational path. The Master's Degree in Management aims to prepare qualified graduates with strong skills in business management, for companies, both national and multinational, non-profit organizations, and public bodies and administrations operating in various sectors.
The aim of the Master's Degree is to train professionals capable of managing the main company functions, in the various contexts, both through full mastery of analysis techniques and knowledge for supporting decision-making processes, and by coordinating and organizing operational units.
The curriculum of the Master's Degree in Management is also designed with the aim of activating a "parallel" degree course (ex DM 50/2010, §31) entirely in English with the same learning objectives and career opportunities. The parallel course, organized and managed in partnership with foreign universities, provides for students to spend significant periods (at least one year) at other universities and obtain a joint degree recognized in various countries, or a double degree.
The internship usually involves a period at a company to address a specific problem of direct interest to the company, under the guidance of a company tutor and an academic tutor.
The degree thesis is an important moment of in-depth study, including theoretical and conceptual aspects, of typical management topics, and is accompanied by an empirical part.
Students who join programs for the award of double degrees or joint degrees with other European universities and spend a period at one or more universities in other countries, follow a program common to all participating students or in any case compatible with their respective educational regulations, with the educational activities carried out at foreign institutions being recognized, based on specific agreements between universities, according to the scheme provided for by the European Credit Transfer System (ECTS). The assessments of the progress tests are converted on the basis of specific conversion tables.
The educational regulations of the study course may provide for the activation of educational paths or curricula.
Knowledge required for access: Enrollment in the Master's Degree in Management is subject to verification of the following requirements:
- curricular requirements:
o possession of a first-level degree, obtained at Italian Universities or another qualification obtained abroad, recognized as suitable;
o at least a B1 level or equivalent proficiency in English;
- specific preparation requirements:
o demonstrate good skills in reading and commenting on data relating to the economic performance of companies and the economic-financial context;
o demonstrate good analytical skills in addressing quantitative problems;
o have adequate familiarity with the basic techniques of descriptive statistics;
o have good analytical skills for economic and business problems.
The knowledge usually includes the equivalent of at least 60 ECTS relating to courses belonging to the basic and characterizing scientific-disciplinary sectors of degree classes 17 and 28 (ex DM. 509/99) or degree classes 18 and 33 (ex DM. 270/04).
Specific disciplinary knowledge requirements are indicated by the educational regulations of the study course, which regulate the verification of the possession of these requirements.
The personal preparation is assessed according to the methods defined by the educational regulations of the study course.
The verification of the possession of these requirements is regulated by the educational regulations of the study course.
For degree courses (parallel) that can be activated entirely in a foreign language and are provided for in this curriculum, the language requirement for access is set at a level of at least B2 in English.
In the case of joint degrees with other universities, the admission requirements may be specified in the educational regulations of the study course in order to meet the requirements required by the curricula of the other countries involved.

UNIVERSITY: University of Trento
COURSE: [0327G] INDUSTRIAL ENGINEERING
TYPE: Bachelor's Degree
SECTION: General Description
URL: https://unitn.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10131

[0327G] INDUSTRIAL ENGINEERING Bachelor's Degree
COURSE INFORMATION General information relating to the current academic year Duration: 3 years Program: 2020 Status: Open Credits: 180 CFU Type: Three-year degree courses Category: Engineering, computer science and technology Regulations: Reform 270/04 Degree class: L-9 - Class of degrees in Industrial Engineering Inter-university Course: No Programmed Access (245 places) Location: Polo di collina - Povo B - via Sommarive, 9 Department: Department of Industrial Engineering Verification of knowledge required for access: No Program, texts and objectives: Specific learning objectives of the course and description of the educational path. The Degree Course trains engineers with in-depth methodological and operational knowledge in the basic scientific disciplines (mathematics, physics, chemistry, computer science) and broad skills in the engineering disciplines, with particular emphasis on those specific to industrial engineering for a modern and advanced approach to the complexity of typical industrial manufacturing problems.
The specific objective of the degree course is the training of system engineers oriented towards the manufacturing industry, who have acquired tools for understanding subsystems of different nature (in particular, mechanical, technological, electrical, electronic and computer science) and strong skills in functional integration; they must also know conventional and innovative materials, the development of related production technologies, the management of industrial processes and plants, and the organization of companies, without neglecting the fundamental skills of information engineering, necessary both to ensure a rational use of the potential offered by information technologies and to understand their impact on production realities.
The Degree Course intends to provide, from different points of view, an overall vision oriented to the life cycle of industrial products and related production processes, in a form useful for developing any material good, including the same tools and production processes, with particular attention to the processes of development of new technologies and new products, their cognitive and connectivity functionalities. A modern methodological approach is favored, according to which the form of a solution follows the function, so as to prepare people who are able to understand the technical problems of engineering, to identify and acquire information and data useful for the analysis and solution of problems, to face even new problems, to expand their knowledge and to generate specific knowledge, to communicate information and solutions clearly.
The natural outcome of the educational path is access to a second-level degree course, although the preparation is also adequate for entering the world of work. In fact, graduates will have acquired solid training and the theoretical, practical and methodological foundations necessary for continuing their education in second-level degree courses, in particular in the field of Mechanical Engineering, Automation Engineering, Chemical Engineering, Materials Engineering and Management Engineering, but not only. A professionalizing path is not foreseen, however, at the end of the three-year period, graduates will have at their disposal interdisciplinary methodological skills essential for direct entry into the world of work. They will be able to operate competently throughout the life cycle of products and can be included in the development processes of new industrial products and new technologies. In particular, the theoretical knowledge and the methodological and operational skills necessary for continuing education in master's degrees are acquired through the study of basic scientific disciplines (mathematics, physics, chemistry and computer science) and by placing particular emphasis on the scientific and methodological aspects of the characterizing disciplines of mechanical engineering, materials engineering, electrical engineering, as well as on the main concepts of information engineering, fundamental in the evolution of industrial processes in the last two decades.
The technological, design and application knowledge and skills important for entering the world of work are acquired by the student in all courses of the disciplines of mechanical engineering, materials engineering and electronics and information engineering, and especially in those most oriented to design and transformation and production technologies.
The educational path is structured over three years, providing a first year mainly dedicated to the teaching of basic disciplines, a second year to complete the basic training to which the main characterizing teachings of industrial engineering are added, which draw on the scientific knowledge acquired with the study of the basic disciplines and provide the methodological and operational knowledge necessary for the completion of the training, which takes place during the third year in which the student can access a wide range of specific teachings of industrial engineering. The third year is completed by elective courses, evenly distributed over the two semesters, and the final exam. Activities dedicated to the development of knowledge useful for entering the world of work and those for knowledge of the English language are evenly distributed over the three years. It is possible to offer students non-compulsory courses in English.
The educational objectives of the degree course will be achieved through the articulation of the educational offer in educational paths that will share all the basic teachings and a solid core of characterizing teachings of industrial engineering. The educational paths will begin to differentiate from the second year of the course, allowing the deepening of specific themes such as the study of materials, production processes and transformation technologies, or the study of the fundamentals of automation, the disciplines of electrical and electronic engineering. The educational paths will be defined in detail in the didactic regulations of the degree course. To guarantee the completeness of the preparation in the disciplines of industrial engineering, in addition to a solid core of characterizing disciplines of mechanical engineering, each educational path, the student will acquire a significant minimum number of credits in the areas of materials engineering and electrical engineering, this to guarantee the primary objective of training system engineers oriented towards the manufacturing industry. The educational paths that will be activated will allow in-depth studies in the various areas, but will still be characterized by a solid common training base in the areas mentioned above.
This preparation makes the graduate in Industrial Engineering particularly suitable to face the continuous challenges in product and process innovation imposed by global competition. Inserted in a work environment organized for groups and projects, or within the scope of autonomous activities, the graduate has the ability to use state-of-the-art design tools and methods, choose and use materials, manage production processes and technologies, conduct experiments and analyze data; he/she has skills in synthesis and communication (also in foreign languages) and understands the social effects of technology.
Knowledge required for access: To enroll in the Degree Course in Industrial Engineering, it is required to have a high school diploma obtained in Italy or another qualification obtained abroad recognized as suitable.
The Degree Course in Industrial Engineering requires adequate skills and knowledge from the candidates, in particular:
- ability to understand verbal communication and to communicate effectively in writing and orally in Italian;
- adequate knowledge of the English language (as established in the Regulations of the Degree Course);
- ability to logical deductive reasoning;
- ability to analyze the behavior of a simple system starting from the fundamental laws and the characteristics of its components;
- ability to distinguish between necessary and sufficient condition;
- ability to distinguish between definition, theorem and demonstration.
Basic knowledge in Mathematics (Arithmetic and algebra, Analytical geometry and numerical functions, Trigonometry, Statistics, computer science), in Physical and chemical sciences (Mechanics, Optics, Thermodynamics, Electromagnetism, Chemistry and structure of matter) is also required.
To access the degree course, candidates must take the entrance test for Engineering organized on a national basis by the Interuniversity Center for Access to Schools of Engineering and Architecture (CISIA) and an English language proficiency test.
The entrance test is designed to determine whether the candidate possesses, and to what extent, the essential knowledge specified above.
The didactic regulations of the degree course will establish the criteria and methods for verifying any additional training obligations and the tools offered to remedy them (DM 270/2004, art. 6, paragraph 1).

UNIVERSITY: University of Trento
COURSE: [0331H] CIVIL ENGINEERING
TYPE: Master's Degree
SECTION: General Description
URL: https://unitn.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10149

[0331H] CIVIL ENGINEERING Master's Degree - Italian/English
COURSE INFORMATION General information relating to the current academic year Duration: 2 years Program: 2023 Status: Open Credits: 120 ECTS Type: Master's Degree Program Category: Engineering, Computer Science and Technology Regulations: Reform 270/04 Degree Class: LM-23 - Master's Degree in Civil Engineering Inter-University Course: No Open Admission: Yes Location: Campus of the Hills - Mesiano - Via Mesiano, 77 (Sustainable capacity: 30) Department: Department of Civil, Environmental and Mechanical Engineering Verification of knowledge required for access: No Program, texts and objectives: Specific learning objectives of the course and description of the educational path. The Master's Degree in Civil Engineering is aimed at training highly qualified professionals who are prepared to address and solve in a coordinated manner the complex design, operational, organizational and management issues that characterize the civil engineering sector or that require an interdisciplinary approach; it consolidates the knowledge acquired in the previous degree program in basic scientific disciplines such as physics, mathematics and in the disciplines of civil engineering, achieving high levels of ability to design, plan, program and manage complex systems, processes and services, in particular in the area of small and large structures, in the area of structural rehabilitation and conservation, in the design of infrastructure works and integrated design with solid knowledge and skills in new digital technologies.
The degree program includes a core of courses common to the first year, with the aim of consolidating the knowledge acquired in the previous degree program and acquiring specific common knowledge in the field of civil engineering, in particular structural engineering, geotechnical engineering and construction science, all in an advanced manner; specific and design knowledge is then transmitted in the characterizing and related courses of the curriculum, starting from the first year, in order to train a graduate capable of moving competently in different thematic areas of civil engineering related to the design of civil engineering works in its various application areas.
The second year is entirely dedicated to specific training, which differs depending on the different areas of choice, but all of which aim to offer students not only theoretical, technical and regulatory knowledge and skills, but also design skills in areas that allow for in-depth study and specialization:
- in the design, analysis, construction and control of structures in civil and industrial fields, with reference to traditional materials (concrete, steel, wood) and advanced materials (composite materials, metamaterials) and with particular attention to exceptional actions (fire, earthquake, NaTech events, etc.);
- in the rehabilitation of civil and industrial structures, with attention to the reduction of seismic vulnerability and the compatibility of interventions with the principles of conservation (reversibility, minimal impact) and environmental sustainability (use of sustainable materials such as wood), also with reference to buildings of historical or monumental interest;
- in the design, construction, management and maintenance of infrastructure works (roads, bridges and hydraulic works), underground structures, retaining structures and foundations, with strong skills in new digital technologies (sensors, telecommunications, data inversion techniques, optimization and artificial intelligence, decision support systems);
- in the integrated design of buildings developed in a digital BIM/HBIM (Building Information Modeling/Heritage Building Information Modeling) environment in the various phases of ideation, construction, management, maintenance and decommissioning, with particular regard to energy saving, living comfort and innovation of technical/technological systems in new and existing civil works.
All profiles will be characterized by specific digital skills, consistent with the professional skills required by the Industrial Revolution 4.0 in the construction sector. These skills include: systems and methods for the monitoring, management and control of civil and infrastructure structures with specific skills in new digital technologies (wireless communication infrastructures, distributed and non-invasive wireless monitoring, data inversion techniques, optimization and artificial intelligence, numerical methods, decision support systems).
The themes of ethics and professional deontology are specifically addressed by professionals in the field, as part of a specific agreement that the Department of Civil, Environmental and Mechanical Engineering has with the professional orders of engineers through seminars proposed within specific teaching activities.
The degree program will offer the appropriate conditions to create a context in which students can be involved in design activities and create moments of comparison, discussion and reflection. Some curricula will offer courses in English, ensuring compliance with the regulations regarding degree programs offered in two languages; for students whose native language is not Italian and who choose a curriculum in English or Italian and English, training activities are planned to acquire the knowledge of Italian required for graduates.
The training provided allows the Civil Engineering graduate to develop scientific and study skills that encourage both individual learning and research and collaborative learning, and that allow graduates to participate in the selections provided for access to the academic career or a professional context.
The training content offered in the curricula is specified in the didactic regulations of the degree program.
Knowledge required for access: Access to the Master's Degree requires the possession of specific curricular requirements and adequate personal preparation, which must be verified. Only candidates who meet the curricular requirements have access to this verification, and it is evaluated on the basis of the results obtained in the previous university career and a possible interview, according to the methods specified in the Didactic Regulations of the Degree Course.
The curricular requirements required for access to the Master's Degree consist of:
- possession of a first-level degree in the degree classes L-7 ex DM 270/04 and Class 8 ex DM 509/99;
or
- possession of a first-level degree in classes other than L-7 ex DM 270/04 and Class 8 ex DM 509/99 or a degree obtained abroad recognized as suitable and having acquired in previous study paths a minimum number of training credits (CFU) in specific scientific-disciplinary groups:
• at least 20 CFU in the SSDs MAT/02, MAT/03, MAT/05, MAT/08;
• at least 18 CFU in the SSDs CHIM/06, CHIM/07, FIS/01, FIS/03, ING-IND/10, ING-IND/11, ING-IND/22;
• at least 9 CFU in ICAR/08, ING-IND/04, ING-IND/13, ING-IND/14.
For all candidates who choose to follow a curriculum offered in Italian and English or only in English, the possession of knowledge at the B2 CEFR level of the English language is verified upon access.
For candidates whose native language is not Italian, who have not completed at least one cycle of higher education in Italian, knowledge of Italian will be verified, unless they choose a curriculum offered entirely in English.

UNIVERSITY: University of Trento
COURSE: [0332H] ENVIRONMENTAL AND TERRITORIAL ENGINEERING
TYPE: Master's Degree
SECTION: General description
URL: https://unitn.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10150

[0332H] ENVIRONMENTAL AND TERRITORIAL ENGINEERING Master's Degree - Italian, English
COURSE INFORMATION General information relating to the current academic year Duration: 2 years - Program: 2021 - Status: Open - Credits: 120 CFU - Type: Master's Degree Courses - Category: Engineering, computer science and technology - Regulations: Reform 270/04
Degree Class: LM-35 - Class of Master's Degrees in Engineering for the Environment and Territory - Inter-university Course: No - Open Access: Yes - Location: Hill Campus - Mesiano - Via Mesiano, 77 (Sustainable capacity: 36)
Department: Department of Civil, Environmental and Mechanical Engineering - Verification of knowledge required for access: No - Program, texts and objectives: Specific educational objectives of the course and description of the educational path. The Master's graduate in Environmental and Territorial Engineering is able to design, plan, design and manage works, systems, plants and services in various areas of interest of civil-environmental engineering, which are explored within specific curricula in different thematic areas, such as:
- the protection of the territory from natural and anthropogenic hazards, the analysis of danger and the mitigation of hydrogeological risk, the protection and rehabilitation of environmental water bodies;
- urban and river hydraulic infrastructures and works, reinforcement and protection works of defense systems, and in general civil works within the territorial context;
- sustainable management of environmental resources, the treatment of emissions in solid, liquid and gaseous form, the monitoring of quality, the prevention of pollution and the remediation of environmental matrices (air, water, soil);
- the management of the territory and the built environment, even in the presence of conflicting uses of resources, with a view to environmental sustainability and within specific socio-economic contexts.
Furthermore, the Master's graduate in Environmental and Territorial Engineering is able to:
- select and therefore use mathematical and numerical models in a conscious way;
- use techniques and technologies for the acquisition and analysis of data for environmental monitoring;
- analyze and interpret environmental data characterized by considerable complexity;
- analyze and evaluate the environmental impact of civil and industrial works and develop procedures for strategic environmental assessment of territorial plans and programs;
- interact with experts from different disciplines in order to solve complex environmental problems.
The educational path includes a core of common lessons, mainly in the first year and taught in English, with the aim of providing the typical skills of the engineer who works in the civil-environmental field, combining them with the ability to use numerical tools for the modeling of environmental processes and the knowledge of the approaches of ecologically oriented territorial planning.
The methods and skills acquired in the common lessons are functional to deepen the more applicative aspects that are addressed in curricula focused on specific themes. The language of the lessons of the curricula varies between Italian and English depending on the characteristics of the theme addressed.
Knowledge required for access: To enroll in the Master's Degree Course in Environmental and Territorial Engineering, it is necessary to have a degree or another qualification obtained abroad recognized as suitable.
Access is direct in the case of a degree or university diploma obtained at the end of a course of study lasting at least three years in the degree class L-7 (DM 270/04) or in class 8 (DM 509/99), or a qualification obtained abroad recognized as suitable.
In the case of a degree or university diploma, obtained at the end of a course of study lasting at least three years, in classes other than those indicated in the previous point or other qualification obtained abroad, access to the course of study is also subject to the possession of the following minimum curricular requirements:
- 24 CFU in the SSDs MAT/03, MAT/05, MAT/06, MAT/07, MAT/08;
- 18 CFU in the SSDs CHIM/06, CHIM/07, FIS/01, FIS/03, ING-IND/10, ING-IND/11, ING-IND/22;
- 30 CFU in the SSDs ICAR/01, ICAR/02, ICAR/03, ICAR/06, ICAR/07;
- 12 CFU in the SSDs ICAR/08, ICAR/09.
Admission to the Master's Degree Course in Environmental and Territorial Engineering is subject to the verification of adequate personal preparation of the student, assessed on the basis of the results obtained in the previous university career and of a possible interview, according to the methods specified in the Didactic Regulations of the Course of Study.
Admission requires knowledge of the English language at a level not lower than B2 of the Common European Framework of Reference.
For students who are not native Italian speakers - or who have not completed a higher education cycle in that language - who intend to choose a curriculum with compulsory lessons in Italian, knowledge of the Italian language at least at level B2 is also required.
Students must have the curricular requirements before the verification of personal preparation.
The assignment of additional educational debts or obligations is not allowed.

SECTION: General description
URL: https://unitn.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10437

[0337H] ENERGY ENGINEERING Master's Degree Inter-university
COURSE INFORMATION General information for the current academic year Duration
2 years Program
2023
Status Open Credits
120 CFU Type Master's degree courses Category Engineering, computer science and technology Regulations Reform 270/04
Degree class LM-30 - Class of Master's Degrees in Energy and Nuclear Engineering Inter-university Course Participating Universities Free University of Bolzano Admission Programmed (50 places)
Location Hill Campus - Mesiano - Via Mesiano, 77 (Sustainable capacity: 50)
Department Department of Civil, Environmental and Mechanical Engineering Verification of knowledge required for admission No Program, texts and objectives Admission procedure For the academic year 2024/25, the available places are distributed as follows:
- 1st session (19.12.2023 – 10.03.2024): for all categories (non-EU citizens residing abroad, EU citizens; non-EU citizens residing in Italy);
- 2nd session (15.04.2024 – 01.07.2024): for EU citizens and non-EU citizens residing in Italy.
5 places are reserved for non-EU citizens and 45 places for EU citizens and equivalent non-EU citizens. If not all places in the 1st session are filled, the remaining places will be offered in the 2nd session.
The application involves submitting the documents indicated in the admission announcement through an online procedure indicated on the university portal (https://offertaformativa.unitn.it/it/lm/ingegneria-energetica/iscriversi).
In addition to verifying language skills and curricular requirements, admission to the course is subject to an assessment of the applicant's previous academic career and the result of an oral interview to assess the adequacy of the student's personal preparation in relation to the educational objectives of the Master's degree course.
The evaluation committee is composed of at least 3 professors from the Free University of Bolzano and the Department of Civil, Environmental and Mechanical Engineering.
The dates and deadlines for participation in the selection process are defined in the admission announcement. The committee evaluates only complete applications submitted within the deadlines set by the announcement and establishes the rankings for non-EU citizens residing abroad and for EU citizens and equivalent citizens, respectively.
The ranking is determined based on the following criteria:
- a maximum of 20/100 points for the coherence between the degree held and the objectives of the Master's degree course in energy engineering;
- a maximum of 60/100 points for the academic curriculum and the outcome of the oral interview;
- a maximum of 10/100 points for proven language skills exceeding the minimum requirements;
- a maximum of 10/100 points for the presentation video;
Candidates who have obtained a score of 50/100 or more are considered eligible.
The rankings are published on the university website https://international.unitn.it/masterenergy/results-and-pre-enrollment-procedures) and are valid only for the academic year for which they were established. Admitted candidates must follow the procedures indicated on the Master's degree course website in the 'Application and Admission' section to confirm their assigned place.

UNIVERSITY: University of Trento
COURSE: [0339G] COMPUTER, COMMUNICATION, AND ELECTRONIC ENGINEERING
TYPE: Bachelor's Degree
SECTION: General Description
URL: https://unitn.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10712

[0339G] COMPUTER, COMMUNICATION, AND ELECTRONIC ENGINEERING Bachelor's Degree - Italian and English
COURSE INFORMATION General information relating to the current academic year Duration: 3 years. Admission year: 2019. Status: Open. Credits: 180 CFU. Type of course: Three-year Bachelor's degree. Category: Engineering, computer science and technology. Regulations: 270/04 Reform. Degree class: L-8 - Class of degrees in Information Engineering. Inter-university course: No. Programmed access (215 places). Location: Povo B - via Sommarive, 9 (Sustainable capacity: 188). Department: Department of Engineering and Science of Information. Verification of knowledge required for access: No. Program, texts and objectives: Specific learning objectives of the course and description of the educational path. The Bachelor's Degree in Computer, Communication, and Electronic Engineering aims to train professionals with general skills in the field of information engineering, and, depending on the student's chosen specialization, specific skills in the areas of telecommunications, electronics, and computer science. These professionals meet the needs of the labor market, which often does not require specialization limited to a single sector, but rather a comprehensive understanding of the systems, methodologies, and technologies of the entire information area, as well as the ability to understand the relationships between the various disciplines and to deal professionally with interdisciplinary problems.
The areas of learning involved in achieving the learning objectives are:
- the area of mathematical and physical disciplines to provide basic mathematical and physical knowledge necessary to correctly apply the scientific and experimental method in problem solving;
- the area of computer science disciplines to offer solid skills in computer programming and data structure management;
- the area of technologies and systems for telecommunications to provide knowledge related to signal processing and information reception and transmission;
- the area of electronic devices and circuits to provide basic skills in the design of digital and analog electronic systems and embedded systems;
- specialization courses for the acquisition of more professional skills.
COURSE STRUCTURE The course of study provides a basic training, common to all engineering degrees, followed by a broad engineering training in the area of information engineering. Subsequently, the course of study is structured in paths in which the student, at his/her own choice, will acquire specific and in-depth training in one of the areas that characterize information engineering, such as computer engineering, telecommunications engineering, or electronic engineering.
In order to allow the student to acquire and integrate the various knowledge required in a harmonious and balanced way, the educational offer is structured in:
1) a basic training in which both the methodological-operational aspects of mathematics and the fundamental knowledge and methodological aspects of the basic sciences of interest for information technologies are dealt with;
2) a broad engineering training in the area of information engineering, in which the fundamental contents of the disciplines that characterize the information area (electronics, computer science and telecommunications) and the knowledge of the related methodologies are acquired.
3) At this point, students can choose between different paths:
3.1) specific and in-depth training in the field of telecommunications and the design of systems for the processing and reception-transmission of information, in order to guarantee a methodological and applicative preparation aimed at the analysis, modeling and design of systems, equipment and infrastructures concerning the acquisition, processing and transmission of information;
3.2) specific and in-depth training in the field of computer science and complex information processing systems, in order to guarantee a methodological and design preparation aimed at the design of hardware and software architectures of products and services for automation and production support;
3.3) specific and in-depth training in the field of electronics and the design of circuits and integrated systems, in order to guarantee a methodological and design preparation aimed at the analysis, modeling, design and control of hardware devices and systems of analog and digital type concerning the methods of acquisition, processing and transmission of information and automation.
4) The training of students will be complemented by a preparation aimed at managing digital transformation and data science in computer, economic, statistical, organizational and management engineering disciplines, which the student can deepen according to his/her interests.
5) Students can then complete their educational path with elective courses, for example, courses in areas other than the one chosen as the main one, or advanced courses.
In order to allow students to acquire an operational knowledge of the English language, the educational path provides for at least three university training credits aimed at achieving an adequate linguistic level for attending teaching activities delivered in English.
The activities proposed in the last year of study also allow the student to choose whether to complete the educational path with methodological content or with project activities or internships aimed at acquiring more professional skills.
The methodological approfondissements are aimed at providing students with the necessary prerequisites to successfully continue their education in a master's degree course or in a first-level master's course and are organized in paths oriented to the disciplines of telecommunications engineering, computer science, electronics and other technical-scientific degrees such as computer science.
The course of study is strongly project-based, using the didactic tools of the laboratories and developing activities for the design of devices and software.
The professional approfondissements also provide for the acquisition of design, technological and operational skills, aimed at direct and effective insertion into the world of work, which can also be acquired through training internships at companies operating in the sector.
Knowledge required for access: To enroll in the Bachelor's Degree in Computer, Communication, and Electronic Engineering, it is required to have a high school diploma obtained in Italy or another qualification obtained abroad recognized as suitable.
For access to the Bachelor's Degree in Computer, Communication, and Electronic Engineering, the following knowledge is also required:
Essential knowledge
- Knowledge of the Italian language
- Adequate knowledge of the English language.
Scientific knowledge
- Arithmetic and algebra: properties and operations on numbers (integers, rational, real); absolute value; powers and roots; logarithms and exponentials; literal calculation; polynomials (operations, factorization); algebraic equations and inequalities of the first and second degree; systems of first-degree equations.
- Geometry: segments and angles (their measure and properties); lines and planes; notable geometric loci; properties of the main plane geometric figures; properties of the main solid geometric figures.
- Analytical geometry and functions: Cartesian coordinates; concept of function; equations of lines and simple geometric loci; graphs and properties of elementary functions.
- Trigonometry: graphs and properties of trigonometric functions; main trigonometric formulas (addition, subtraction, duplication, bisection); relationships between the elements of a triangle.
Physics and Chemistry
- Knowledge of the elementary notions on physical quantities and the structure of matter.
The didactic regulations describe in more detail the specific knowledge required for access to the Bachelor's Degree and specify the methods of verification of this knowledge; any additional training obligations to be completed within the first year of the course will also be specified in the event that the verification is not positive.

UNIVERSITY: University of Trento
COURSE: [0342H] ARTIFICIAL INTELLIGENCE SYSTEMS
TYPE: Master's Degree
SECTION: General Description
URL: https://unitn.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10738

[0342H] ARTIFICIAL INTELLIGENCE SYSTEMS Master's Degree - English
COURSE INFORMATION General information relating to the current academic year. Duration: 2 years. Program: 2020. Status: Open. Credits: 120 CFU. Type of Course: Master's Degree. Category: Engineering, Computer Science and Technology. Regulations: Reform 270/04. Degree Class: LM-32 - Class of Master's Degrees in Computer Engineering. Inter-University Course: No. Limited Access (80 places). Location: Povo B - via Sommarive, 9 (Sustainable capacity: 80). Department: Department of Engineering and Information Science. Verification of knowledge required for access: No. Program, texts and objectives: Specific learning objectives of the course and description of the educational path. SPECIFIC LEARNING OBJECTIVES: Graduates in Artificial Intelligence Systems will:
i) be able to develop and use innovative methods and tools to address emerging problems, designing, engineering, organizing and managing complex and innovative systems based on intelligent computer systems; to this end, they will have to acquire a broad set of skills focused on the field of Computer Engineering but extended to interdisciplinary application contexts;
ii) possess a solid theoretical-scientific culture of mathematics and information engineering;
iii) be able to identify the connections between ethical principles and legal rules, as well as understand the implications of the use of technologies and the impact of their design choices.
Furthermore, based on the chosen educational orientation, they will be able to expand their interdisciplinary skills, achieving one of the following objectives in addition to those listed above:
1) further deepen their theoretical and methodological knowledge in order to achieve a high degree of specialization in the field of design of systems based on artificial intelligence;
2) acquire advanced knowledge in the various disciplines of information engineering for various industrial, environmental and biomedical applications;
3) propose strategies for bringing to market products, processes and organizations based on artificial intelligence and competitive in terms of efficiency, productivity and sustainability;
4) integrate computational, behavioral approaches and neuroimaging technologies for a better understanding of human action and to provide inspiration for the development of new intelligent systems.
DESCRIPTION OF THE EDUCATIONAL PATH: The educational path of the Master's Degree in Artificial Intelligence Systems is entirely taught in English. It includes common activities in the following areas of learning:
i) foundational and applied computer science disciplines of artificial intelligence, such as knowledge representation, automatic reasoning, machine learning, natural language processing, optimization, human-computer interaction;
ii) disciplines related to industrial and service robotics, the operational autonomy of mechatronic devices, and the automation of complex processes;
iii) disciplines relating to the acquisition, processing and analysis of information (signals, voice, images and video) and computer vision and their applications;
iv) disciplines relating to the legal area, with particular reference to the basic knowledge of the legal systems relevant to the design and application of artificial intelligence systems.
After a substantial period of common activities, the educational offer is divided into several orientations, which will be defined in the Didactic Regulations of the study course. In particular:
1) The first orientation provides for further in-depth study in the mathematical and/or computer science disciplines that are foundational and applied to artificial intelligence and those related to robotics.
2) The second orientation aims to provide more in-depth training in the disciplines of information engineering for the design and use of artificial intelligence systems in various application contexts.
3) The third orientation opens up to disciplines related to the economic and management area, for the organization, management and innovation of decision and production systems based on artificial intelligence.
4) Finally, the last orientation is based on disciplines of cognitive neuroscience, language, and their applications to artificial intelligence models.
The educational path offered to the student includes substantial laboratory activities, focusing on various application and industrial domains, projects, seminars and company internships, which promote the evaluation and development of real systems and the communication and autonomous learning skills considered essential in a professional profile related to scientific and technological innovation in artificial intelligence.
Knowledge required for access: To access the Master's Degree in Artificial Intelligence Systems, it is necessary to have the curricular requirements and adequate personal preparation as specified below.
Curricular requirements:
1. possession of a degree in one of the following classes L-8, L-9, L-31 or other degree obtained abroad recognized as suitable;
1.bis if the first-level degree held is in classes other than those indicated in point 1), it is required to have acquired at least 36 CFU in the group of sectors: CHIM/03, CHIM/06-07, FIS/01-03, MAT/01-09, SECS-S/01
2. having acquired at least 12 CFU in the scientific-disciplinary sectors INF/01 or ING-INF/05.
Requirements for personal preparation:
The candidate must demonstrate that they possess:
1) basic knowledge and skills sufficient to allow them to attend the training activities provided for in the aforementioned Master's Degree, according to the methods defined in the Didactic Regulations of the Study Course;
2) knowledge of the English language at a level of at least B2 (according to the CEFR);
- The didactic regulations indicate the required level of knowledge of the Italian language and the related methods of verification. Students can include in their study plan activities aimed at achieving these linguistic skills;
- The verification of personal preparation is mandatory; any curricular integrations must be acquired before the verification of personal preparation;
- The methods of verification of personal preparation are outlined in the didactic regulations of the study course.

UNIVERSITY: University of Trento
COURSE: [0343H] MANAGEMENT AND INDUSTRIAL SYSTEMS ENGINEERING
TYPE: Master's Degree
SECTION: General Description
URL: https://unitn.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10749

[0343H] MANAGEMENT AND INDUSTRIAL SYSTEMS ENGINEERING Master's Degree - Italian
COURSE INFORMATION General information relating to the current academic year Duration: 2 years. Admission year: 2021. Status: Open. Credits: 120 CFU. Type: Master's degree courses. Category: Engineering, computer science and technology. Regulations: Reform 270/04. Degree class: LM-31 - Class of Master's Degrees in Management Engineering. Inter-university course: No. Open access: Yes. Location: Hill Campus - Povo B - via Sommarive, 9. Department: Department of Industrial Engineering. Verification of knowledge required for access: No. Program, texts and objectives: Specific educational objectives of the course and description of the educational path. The educational path aims to respond to the strong demand for skills expressed by the world of work and, in particular, by the industrial sector, where engineers are increasingly called upon to:
- manage complex systems with a holistic vision, ensuring attention to the various factors that contribute to determining overall performance in the industrial field;
- design, manage and improve industrial processes, both of a production and logistics nature, and of an organizational nature;
- manage technological and organizational innovation by adopting multi- and interdisciplinary approaches;
- promote the development of new production and economic paradigms deriving from the cognitive and connectivity potential offered by recent developments in digital technologies, while managing the profound changes in roles and operational contexts that result from them;
- combine technical knowledge and skills with economic and organizational knowledge and skills.
To this end, the Master's Degree course in Management and Industrial Systems Engineering provides a solid, but at the same time balanced, competence in both technical disciplines and disciplines related to the management of the systemic complexity of modern industry, also considering economic-organizational aspects and the challenges deriving from the environmental sustainability of industrial development. In particular, the course of study provides the graduate with the intellectual tools to:
- apply the tools and methodologies for the design and management of complex systems;
- apply modern quantitative techniques to support decision-making;
- manage the main digital technologies underlying the new industrial revolution;
- apply methodologies for promoting and managing innovation;
- evaluate the environmental sustainability of industrial products and processes, with a focus on a holistic and integrated vision of problems rather than on specific aspects or technologies.
The training content will be aligned with the recent technological and organizational developments that are revolutionizing the world of industry, from the personalization of products to the environmental sustainability of processes, from the digitalization of industrial plants to the quality and safety of production systems.
The student will also have the opportunity to complete their training through practical training and/or internships at companies, professional firms and other public and private bodies.
Based on this consolidated structure, based on solid training in the disciplines that characterize management engineering and industrial systems, differentiated training paths are also defined in order to allow the student to integrate their training on thematic areas characteristic of industrial engineering or information engineering, always in compliance with the constraints imposed by the didactic regulations.
At the end of the educational path, the graduate will have solid knowledge regarding the methodologies for the design and management of industrial plants, logistics systems and production processes, combined with managerial skills necessary for the organization and development of various company functions, such as research and development, and marketing. In addition to these skills, the graduates will have acquired relevant knowledge in the field of innovation project management, quality and safety of industrial systems, digital technologies enabling production systems, with particular regard to the automation and control of industrial processes.
The graduates will be able to use these skills in an appropriate, interdisciplinary and innovative way, always taking into account the high rate of dynamism that characterizes modern industrial systems. The graduates will be able to effectively interpret the rapid organizational changes and the constant technological evolution typical of the current period of industrial development. The graduates will have solid engineering and technological skills that will allow them to fully understand the changes taking place and guide them towards configurations that are more beneficial for the system in which they will operate. In particular, the graduates will have the opportunity to develop knowledge in various areas relevant to modern industrial systems, such as the economic, environmental and social sustainability of production processes, the digitalization of processing and logistics systems, the modeling and optimization of business and industrial processes, as well as the management of complex and interdisciplinary projects, organizing the related multifunctional teams.
The graduates will have acquired specific in-depth knowledge in various disciplines of mechanical industrial plants, economic and management engineering, processing and system technologies, industrial automation and in some related disciplines that will contribute to consolidate the technical preparation and increase the interaction skills of the graduates with the various company functions throughout the product design and development phase, in the management of its life cycle, in the selection of materials and related production and transformation technologies, in the definition of optimization strategies, in the selection of digital technologies enabling production systems.
In particular, the training will include the in-depth study of product design methods and production, logistics, storage and warehousing systems, strategies related to organizational structure, methodologies for customized production, process control and intelligent product manufacturing, and finally, modeling of complex systems and analysis of dynamic networks.
The training framework is completed by the possibility offered to the student to choose between differentiated training paths, which will allow them to complete their preparation by deepening the study on topics of sectors related to management engineering, with emphasis on the thematic areas characteristic of industrial engineering and information engineering.
The training program concludes with a final test which involves the preparation of an original thesis.
The training activity may include laboratory experiences carried out within specific courses, group work, seminars and company internships.
Knowledge required for access: The curricular requirements and the adequacy of the personal preparation for access to the Master's Degree Course, pursuant to Article 6, paragraph 2, of DM 270/2004, are established on the basis of the curricular and preparation criteria illustrated below.
Curricular requirements
• Graduates in class L-9 (ex DM 270/2004) who have acquired:
- at least 12 CFU in the scientific-disciplinary sectors (SSD) of the disciplinary areas characteristic of Management Engineering: ING-IND/16, ING-IND/17, ING-IND/35, ING-INF/04.
• Graduates in other degree classes who have acquired in the first-level study path:
- at least 24 CFU in the SSD: MAT/02, MAT/03, MAT/05, MAT/06, MAT/07, MAT/08, SECS-S/01, SECS-S/02, SECS-S/06;
- at least 15 CFU in the SSD: FIS/01, FIS/03, FIS/07, CHIM/03, CHIM/06, CHIM/07;
- at least 12 CFU in the SSD: ING-IND/16, ING-IND/17, ING-IND/35, ING-INF/04;

• For those with other degree titles obtained abroad recognized as suitable, the verification of the curricular requirements is carried out by a commission appointed by the Department Council, considering appropriate equivalencies between the contents of the exams taken in the previous study path and those corresponding to the areas and disciplinary sectors specified above.

COURSE: [0344H] MECHATRONICS ENGINEERING
TYPE: Master's Degree
SECTION: General Description
URL: https://unitn.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10752

[0344H] MECHATRONICS ENGINEERING Master's Degree - Italian
COURSE INFORMATION General information relating to the current academic year Duration: 2 years. Admission year: 2021. Status: Open. Credits: 120 CFU. Type of course: Master's degree. Category: Engineering, computer science and technology. Regulations: Reform 270/04. Degree class: LM-33 - Class of Master's Degrees in Mechanical Engineering. Inter-university course: No. Open access: Yes. Location: Polo di collina - Povo B - via Sommarive, 9 (Sustainable capacity: 35). Department: Department of Industrial Engineering. Verification of knowledge required for access: No. Program, texts and objectives: Specific educational objectives of the course and description of the educational path. The general educational objective of the degree program is to promote a culture of innovation in all aspects of the professional activities of the mechatronic engineer. The objectives of the LM33 class constitute the high-level references that, in this degree program, are instrumental in providing the capacity for innovation in the design, planning, design and management of systems, processes and services. Basic sciences are primarily interpreted as disciplines aimed at the functional description of the mechatronic system, understood as a set of subsystems that interact at multiple levels (mechanical, electronic, computer) and that increasingly integrate cognitive and interconnection capabilities. The training aims to develop transversal skills that allow the student to organize the necessary connections between disciplines, understanding the experimental purpose (through physical laboratories and/or simulations) of the learning process.
The organization of the educational path is based on a first year aimed at providing students with the fundamental theoretical tools of numerical analysis and mechatronics (mechanics, automation, signals and measurements), consolidating the ability to understand, formalize, model, simulate and control an intelligent mechanical system. The second year completes the training by focusing on specific in-depth studies of advanced mechanics, dynamics, distributed systems, robotics, innovative control applications and intelligent transport systems, organized according to the curricula of the degree program.
From the general objective, the educational objectives of the various curricula of the degree program derive, which are aimed at equipping students with tools and methods to successfully carry out the profession of the modern mechatronic engineer. The curricula focus on application contexts of different areas, considered strategic and in line with the identity of the training project. Among these declinations of the training project, the innovative mechanics (and the related tools for design, verification and control), robotics (from enabling technologies to industrial applications and innovative developments), intelligent vehicles (from autonomous driving to advanced transport systems), and autonomous systems (integrated with aspects of entrepreneurial innovation) stand out.
The professional profiles of the degree program decline the general profile of the system engineer in the specificity of the modern mechatronic field. The most recent developments in advanced industrial reality, identified in the trends of Industry 4.0, increasingly require professional profiles capable of integrating skills from traditionally different areas. The reference profiles are inspired by interdisciplinary figures essential for successfully carrying out numerous professional activities both in manufacturing or service companies, and in public administrations and research institutions, and in freelance or entrepreneurial activities. The figure of the mechatronic engineer at the basis of the design of the degree program was born with the idea of integrating the disciplines related to mechanics and electronics-automation, raising the perspective of study to the entire system rather than its components. The general educational objective of the degree program remains to promote a culture of innovation in all aspects of professional activity.
Graduates of the Master's Degree in Mechatronics Engineering have mastery of the main reference methods for the design, development, production and management of modern Cyber Physical Systems and know how to apply them appropriately, addressing their intrinsic interdisciplinarity, using appropriate theoretical tools and application skills. They know how to apply these methods to the various functional subsystems, defining the interfaces and maintaining the overall vision, appropriately described by engineering requirements. They know how to make use of modern methods of updating knowledge and understand their impact on existing technologies, as well as having innovative tools capable of increasing their competitiveness. They know how to convert the trends of technological and scientific advancement into innovation projects and/or in the conception of new products and processes. They have the necessary skills to apply the technologies of Artificial Intelligence to modern mechatronic products, with particular attention to intelligent vehicles.
Knowledge required for access: The curricular requirements and the adequacy of the personal preparation for access to the Master's Degree course, pursuant to art. 6, paragraph 2, of DM 270/2004, are established on the basis of the curricular and preparation criteria illustrated below.
Curricular requirements:
• Those who have a degree in class L-9 (ex DM 270/2004) and have obtained at least 18 CFU in the following scientific-disciplinary sectors (SSD): ING-IND/12, ING-IND/13, ING-IND/14, ING-INF/01, ING-INF/04, MAT/07 can access the course.
• Those who have a degree in other classes and have obtained at least 24 CFU in the SSDs: MAT/02, MAT/03, MAT/05, MAT/06, MAT/07, MAT/08, SECS-S/06; at least 15 CFU in the SSDs: FIS/01, FIS/02, FIS/03, CHIM/03, CHIM/06, CHIM/07; at least 18 CFU in the SSDs: ING-IND/12, ING-IND/13, ING-IND/14, ING-INF/01, ING-INF/04, ING-INF/07 can access the course.
• For those who hold another degree obtained abroad and recognized as suitable, the verification of the curricular requirements is carried out by a commission appointed by the Department Council, considering appropriate equivalences between the contents of the exams taken in the previous study path and those corresponding to the areas and disciplinary sectors specified above.
Adequacy of personal preparation: The adequacy of personal preparation must be verified. Only candidates who meet the curricular requirements have access to this verification.
The methods of verifying personal preparation are defined in the didactic regulations of the course.
In order to be admitted to the master's degree course, the student must also demonstrate that they have knowledge of the English language at a level equal to at least level B2 (CEFR).

TYPE: Master's Degree
