[0345H] MATERIALS ENGINEERING Master's Degree - Italian
COURSE INFORMATION General information relating to the current academic year Duration: 2 years. Admission: 2022. Status: Open. Credits: 120 CFU. Type of course: Master's degree. Category: Engineering, computer science and technology. Regulations: Reform 270/04. Degree class: LM-53 - Materials Engineering. Inter-university course: No. Open access: Yes. Location: Povo B - via Sommarive, 9 (Sustainable capacity: 35). Department: Department of Industrial Engineering. Verification of knowledge required for access: No. Program, texts and objectives: Specific educational objectives of the course and description of the educational path. The general educational objective of the degree program is to train graduates with solid cultural foundations in the engineering field and with specific knowledge related to the production processes, properties, development and applications of materials, the design, development and innovation of products, the management of the product life cycle in line with the current evolution of modern manufacturing industry, where a systems approach prevails, which is necessary for the application of sustainability criteria and for the implementation of the principles of the circular economy.
The educational path aims to achieve the following specific objectives:
- provide in-depth knowledge of materials science and engineering;
- ensure the acquisition of mastery of the main reference methods for the design, development, production, processing, characterization, quality control and management of traditional and innovative materials for specific applications in the manufacturing industry;
- provide a strong culture of innovation and sensitivity for designing, developing and managing products and related production processes with attention to the entire life cycle;
- provide skills in conducting and coordinating innovation and development projects for materials and industrial products, with a systems vision and a focus on the application of emerging technologies;
- develop the ability to make use of modern methods of updating knowledge, innovative tools capable of increasing its competitiveness and understanding its impact on existing technologies;
- develop the ability to translate trends in technological and scientific development into innovation projects and/or the design of new products and processes for modern industry, in particular the manufacturing sector;
- provide the skills to intervene with a strong innovative vision in key areas of modern industry, such as energy, biomedical technologies, production processes and eco-sustainable materials;
- develop the ability to harmonize the technical-specialistic skills acquired with transversal, non-disciplinary skills (including, for example, knowing how to work in a group, thinking creatively, knowing how to communicate with specific professional figures, knowing how to express oneself and write technical reports in English) and digital skills (including, for example, knowing how to use modeling and design software, carry out online data/information research);
- develop the skills and knowledge that allow to organize the necessary connections between disciplines, understanding the technical and experimental purposes of the learning process also through practical laboratory, design and simulation activities.
The achievement of the educational objectives provided for by the degree program takes place through an educational path in which, in the first common year, the fundamental theoretical-practical tools relating to the chemistry and physics of condensed matter, thermodynamics, the structure and properties of the various classes of materials, the related production processes are provided within compulsory and characterizing educational activities, consolidating the ability to understand their functioning, design their application, evaluate their degradation in use, and plan their restoration.
The second year provides for specific educational activities relating to advanced materials, modern production technologies, methods for product design and development, strategies for managing materials throughout their life cycle, with particular attention to sustainability and recycling. In particular, the courses offered in both the characterizing and related disciplines allow to consolidate the basic skills of the materials engineer on the different classes of materials, on the related manufacturing processes, on the advanced and "smart" properties and applications and allow to acquire the interdisciplinary skills that contribute to consolidate the technical preparation and increase the ability of interaction of the graduates within the various company functions throughout the product design and development phase, as well as in the management of its life cycle and in the definition of enabling technologies for production systems.
In the second year, the student is also offered the possibility of choosing between differentiated in-depth curricula which, integrating aspects of innovation and entrepreneurship, focus on application contexts of different areas, considered strategic and fully consistent with the overall educational project of the degree program. In particular, in-depth studies on manufacturing processes and product development, on the sustainable use of energy, on materials and technologies for biomedical and functional applications and on sustainable materials are highlighted.
Knowledge required for access: The curricular requirements and the adequacy of the personal preparation for access to the Master's Degree course, pursuant to art. 6, paragraph 2, of DM 270/2004, are established on the basis of the curricular and personal preparation criteria illustrated below.
Curricular requirements: Access to the Master's Degree Course in Materials Engineering requires the possession of a degree or other qualification obtained abroad and recognized as suitable.
Graduates who have obtained:
- at least 24 CFU in the SSDs: MAT/02, MAT/03, MAT/05, MAT/06, MAT/07, MAT/08, ING-INF/05;
- at least 18 CFU in the SSDs: FIS/01, FIS/03, CHIM/03, CHIM/06, CHIM/07;
- at least 45 CFU in the SSDs: ING-IND/03, ING-IND/04, ING-IND/05, ING-IND/06, ING-IND/07, ING-IND/08, ING-IND/09, ING-IND/11, ING-IND/12, ING-IND/13, ING-IND/14, ING-IND/15, ING-IND/16, ING-IND/17, ING-IND/19, ING-IND/21, ING-IND/22, ING-IND/23, ING-IND/24, ING-IND/25, ING-IND/26, ING-IND/27, ING-IND/31, ING-IND/32, ING-IND/34, ING-IND/35, ICAR/01, ICAR/08, of which at least 12 CFU in the subgroup of SSDs characterizing materials engineering: ING-IND/16, ING-IND/21, ING-IND/22, ING-IND/23, ING-IND/24, ING-IND/27 and ICAR/08.
For those who hold another qualification obtained abroad and recognized as suitable, the verification of the curricular requirements is carried out by a commission appointed by the Department Council, considering appropriate equivalences between the contents of the exams taken in previous university courses and those corresponding to the areas and disciplinary sectors specified above.
Adequacy of personal preparation: The adequacy of personal preparation must be verified. Only candidates who meet the curricular requirements have access to this verification.
The methods for verifying personal preparation are defined in the didactic regulations of the course.
In order to be admitted to the master's degree course, the student must also demonstrate that they have knowledge of the English language at a level equal to at least level B2 (CEFR).
For students whose native language is not Italian, knowledge of the Italian language will be verified. The methods of verification will be defined in the didactic regulations of the course, where the training activities to be included in the study plan to acquire the knowledge of Italian required for graduates will also be indicated in the event of a negative outcome of this verification.

COURSE: [0346H] INFORMATION ENGINEERING
TYPE: Master's Degree

URL: https://unitn.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10756

[0346H] INFORMATION ENGINEERING Master's Degree - English
COURSE INFORMATION General information relating to the current academic year Duration: 2 years Admission: 2023 Status: Open Credits: 120 CFU Type: Master's Degree Course Category: Engineering, Computer Science and Technology Regulations: Reform 270/04 Degree Class: LM-27 - Class of Master's Degrees in Telecommunications Engineering Inter-University Course: No Programmed Access (80 places) Location: Polo di collina - Povo B - via Sommarive, 9 (Sustainable capacity: 80) Department: Department of Engineering and Science of Information Verification of knowledge required for access: No Program, texts and objectives Specific learning objectives of the course and description of the educational path SPECIFIC LEARNING OBJECTIVES The Master's Degree graduates in Information Engineering will be able to:
i) design, develop and manage complex systems for the acquisition, processing, and extraction of information, as well as the transmission, storage, and protection of data; to this end, they will acquire a broad set of skills focused on the field of Telecommunications Engineering, but extended to interdisciplinary application contexts;
ii) possess a solid technical-scientific culture in information engineering, as well as in mathematics acquired in a cross-cutting manner in compulsory courses;
iii) integrate engineering skills with elements of business organization related to the development, innovation, and management of the company;
iv) thanks to a training program entirely in English, they will have in-depth knowledge of the technical-scientific terminology of the sector, in oral and written form.
Furthermore, based on the chosen educational orientation, they will be able to expand their interdisciplinary skills through the in-depth study of, for example, one of the following objectives:
1) further theoretical and methodological knowledge in order to achieve a high degree of specialization in the design of telecommunications systems;
or acquire advanced knowledge in the disciplines of information engineering:
2) disciplines of computer engineering for the design of artificial intelligence and distributed information systems;
3) disciplines of electronic engineering for the development of electronic systems for signal acquisition and advanced computing architectures;
4) disciplines of biomedical engineering for the acquisition, processing, and automatic analysis of biomedical signals and data.
DESCRIPTION OF THE EDUCATIONAL PATH The educational path of the Master's Degree in Information Engineering includes common activities in the following areas of learning:
i) disciplines related to communication systems and telecommunications networks and their applications;
ii) disciplines related to systems for the acquisition and processing of information (signals, voice, images, and video) and their applications;
iii) disciplines related to systems for the analysis and automatic recognition of information and their applications;
iv) disciplines related to the economic and management area with a view to innovation in the business context.
After a significant period of common activities, the training offer is divided into several orientations, which will be defined in the Didactic Regulations of the study course. These orientations include:
- further in-depth study of the fundamental and applied disciplines of telecommunications engineering, such as communication systems, new generation telecommunications networks, simulation techniques, satellite and radar systems;
- acquisition of more specific training in the disciplines of computer engineering, such as artificial intelligence systems, distributed systems, and IoT;
- an opening to disciplines related to the electronic field, in particular the study and development of sensors for signal acquisition and processing, and advanced computing architectures with high performance;
- the study of disciplines in the biomedical field, such as bioengineering, the processing and management of biomedical data.
The educational path offered to students includes significant laboratory activities, focusing on various application and industrial domains, projects, seminars, and company internships, which promote the evaluation and development of real systems, communication skills, and autonomous learning, activities dedicated to so-called challenge-based learning, as well as renewed attention to soft skills, considered essential in a professional profile related to scientific and technological innovation in the field of information engineering.
Knowledge required for access To enroll in the Master's Degree Course in Information Engineering, it is necessary to have a Bachelor's Degree or another qualification obtained abroad and recognized as suitable.
Access is subject to the possession of curricular requirements and adequate personal preparation, as described below.
Those who have a degree in class L-8, or who have taken a number of training credits in specific groups of CFU at least equal to the minimums indicated, summarized below, have the necessary curricular requirements to access the Master's Degree course:
- 36 CFU in the group CHIM/03, CHIM/07, FIS/01-03, INF/01, MAT/02-03, MAT/05-09
- 24 CFU in the group ING-INF/01-07
For those with other qualifications recognized as suitable, the verification of curricular requirements is carried out by a commission appointed annually, which is responsible for evaluating the curricula of students, based on the relevance of the qualification and the educational path taken in the previous course of studies, as well as the results of the individual exams taken.
The student must have the curricular requirements before the verification of personal preparation; in particular, the assignment of educational debts or obligations is not allowed.
Adequacy of personal preparation:
To access the Master's Degree course, the student must have proven knowledge of the English language at a level of at least B2 (CEFR). The methods of verifying personal preparation are outlined in the didactic regulations of the study course, which also regulate the methods of verifying knowledge of the English language.
For students whose native language is not Italian, knowledge of the Italian language will be verified. The methods of verification will be defined in the Didactic Regulations of the Study Course, where the training activities to be included in the study plan to acquire the knowledge of Italian required for graduates will also be indicated in the event of a negative outcome of this verification.

COURSE: [0347H] BIOENGINEERING FOR PERSONALIZED MEDICINE
TYPE: Master's Degree
CURRICULUM: Inter-university Joint Degree
SECTION: General Description
URL: https://unitn.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10761

[0347H] BIOENGINEERING FOR PERSONALIZED MEDICINE Master's Degree Inter-university Joint Degree - Italian
COURSE INFORMATION General information relating to the current academic year Duration: 2 years Admission: 2024 Approval date: 18-3-2024 Status: Open Credits: 120 ECTS Type: Master's Degree Program Category: Engineering, Computer Science and Technology Regulations: Reform 270/04 Degree Class: LM-21 R - Biomedical Engineering Inter-university Course Participating Universities: University of Modena and Reggio Emilia, University of Verona Admission: Open Access Location: Hill Campus - Rovereto - Via Fortunato Zeni, 8 (Sustainable capacity: 80) Department: Department of Industrial Engineering Verification of knowledge required for admission: No Program, texts and objectives Specific learning objectives of the course and description of the educational path The general objective of the Master's Degree program is to train graduates with solid knowledge and skills for the development, experimentation and management of technologies, instrumentation and devices for biomedical applications and for the analysis and modeling of the complexity of physiological and healthcare systems.
Taking advantage of the possibility of providing additional courses compared to those provided for by the Degree Class (MUR Decree of December 19, 2023), the program extends the characterizing activities with respect to the reference SSDs ING-INF/06 and ING-IND/34, adding two preparatory disciplines to consolidate the engineering skills useful for the development of technologies and tools for advanced biomedical applications with reference to the area of materials and electronics for bio and electro-medical applications. To this end, the courses of Biomedical Engineering: ING-IND/34 and ING-INF/06 are identified as characterizing elements of the Master's Degree program, for a total of 39 ECTS. These are supplemented by the engineering disciplines of science and technology of materials (ING-IND/22) and electronics (ING-INF/01), for a total of 12 ECTS, as basic elements for consolidating knowledge in the areas of industrial engineering and computer and electronics engineering, which constitute the training pillars of the new program. The courses of biomedical disciplines relating to the themes of physiology and biology and medical disciplines also represent an essential complementary training, for the development of skills aimed at the development of technologies and tools for personalized and precision medicine. Finally, the multi- and interdisciplinary training will be guaranteed by a set of related and integrative activities aimed at consolidating engineering skills in the areas of biomechanics, computer science, medical robotics and fluid dynamics.
The set of these courses will provide the graduate with the fundamental skills for the development and evaluation of medical instrumentation, devices and implants, on biomaterials and related advanced manufacturing methods, the processing of biomedical data and signals, the physiology of systems, the principles of diagnostics for personalized medicine and the representation of medical-biological knowledge through advanced modeling. These skills constitute the knowledge base for the development of enabling technologies for modern patient-centered medicine. The graduate of the program will also have acquired knowledge in the field of business organization, innovation processes and professional ethics.
The courses of the program also offer specific in-depth studies that guide the educational path towards technologies for prevention, precision diagnostics, therapeutic, rehabilitative and assistive interventions, addressing the themes of information processing (data, signals, images), the study of advanced technologies for the development of regenerative and precision medicine and aimed at the development of devices, systems, services and processes centered on the citizen/patient (personalized medicine).
The training process will be carried out through lectures, meetings with experts and seminars, exercises in the classroom and in the Department's laboratories, with the involvement of companies and healthcare organizations for the development of application projects based on real scenarios and cases. These project activities, proposed within the framework of the statutory courses of the degree program, will serve to promote technical/scientific in-depth study, knowledge of business rules and career guidance. The internship and the degree thesis complete the training process.
The educational path will always highlight the state of the art of the various disciplines covered and the open challenges, highlighting the continuous evolution of technology and the need for its continuous updating also in relation to the themes of environmental, economic and social sustainability of innovation. To promote these objectives, the course of study may organize specific seminars on topics of particular interest and meetings with the world of work and internships in companies aimed at the degree thesis activity, both on technical topics and on those more closely related to recruitment.
The educational path aims to pursue the following specific objectives:
- provide in-depth and operational knowledge of the development paths of innovation in the biomedical and health technology sectors, with a focus on sustainability and the safety of processes;
- develop the ability to analyze and model complex systems;
- acquire mastery of the main reference methods for the design, development, characterization and management of medical devices, systems and services for biomedical applications;
- provide a strong culture of innovation and the ability to assess its multidimensional impact;
- provide skills in the management and coordination of research and development projects of innovative products, systems and services;
- provide specific skills in the field of signal, data processing and information extraction for diagnostic, therapeutic, rehabilitative and assistive purposes;
- develop awareness of the social role of innovation in healthcare and the profession of Biomedical Engineer;
- provide skills in the technologies of materials for biomedical applications and biofabrication
- provide the basic skills for the development of Healthcare 4.0
- provide the basic knowledge of diagnostics and therapy for personalized medicine Knowledge required for access: To access the program, a Bachelor's or University Diploma of at least three years' duration, or another qualification obtained abroad and recognized as suitable, and the possession of specific curricular requirements are required. Adequate personal preparation is also required, which must be verified. In any case, the assignment of additional educational debts or obligations is not provided.
The curricular requirements and the adequacy of the personal preparation for access to the Master's Degree course in Bioengineering for Personalized Medicine are established on the basis of the curricular and personal preparation criteria illustrated below.
The Master's Degree represents an excellent path for graduates of the first level of the classes L-8 Electronic and Computer Engineering and L-9 Industrial Engineering (ex DM 270/2004). Therefore, graduates in the classes L-8 and L-9 (ex DM 270/2004) can access it.
Graduates in other degree classes with a solid background in basic scientific disciplines can also access the course. In particular, it is required that they have acquired at least 75 ECTS divided into the following groups:
- 24 ECTS in the SSDs: MAT/02, MAT/03, MAT/05, MAT/06, MAT/07, MAT/08, SEC-S/01, SECS-S/02, SECS-S/06;
- 15 ECTS in the SSDs: FIS/01, FIS/03, FIS/07, CHIM/03, CHIM/06, CHIM/07;
- 36 ECTS in the SSDs: ING-IND/06, ING-IND/08, ING-IND/09, ING-IND/10, ING-IND/11, ING-IND/12, ING-IND/13, ING-IND/14, ING-IND/15, ING-IND/21, ING-IND/22, INGIND/23, ING-IND/31, ING-IND/32, ING-IND/34, ING-IND/35, ING-INF/01, ING-INF/02, ING-INF/03, ING-INF/04, ING-INF/05, ING-INF/06, ING-INF/07, ICAR/01, ICAR/08, SECS-P/08.
For those who hold another degree obtained abroad and recognized as suitable, the verification of the curricular requirements is carried out by a commission appointed by the Department Council, considering appropriate equivalences between the contents of the exams taken in the previous study path and those corresponding to the areas and disciplinary sectors specified above.
Adequacy of personal preparation: The methods for verifying personal preparation are defined in the didactic regulations of the course.
For admission to the Master's Degree program, the candidate must demonstrate, in addition to the curricular requirements, that they possess knowledge of the English language at a level equal to at least level B2 (CEFR).
For students whose native language is other than Italian, knowledge of the Italian language at level A1-CEFR will be verified, according to the methods described in the Department Regulations: "Knowledge of the Italian language at the required level is required to be able to take the exams of the second year of the course, and with this verification, 3 ECTS of other training activities are recognized. The credits relating to the Italian language test can also be directly assigned to students who have a valid certification of an equivalent or higher level issued by an official body recognized by the Department or the Language Center (CLA) of the University of Trento. The test is carried out by language experts working at the Language Center (CLA), according to methods agreed with the Department."

UNIVERSITY: University of Trento
COURSE: [0424H] PHILOLOGY AND LITERARY CRITICISM
TYPE: Master's Degree
SECTION: General description
URL: https://unitn.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10166

[0424H] PHILOLOGY AND LITERARY CRITICISM Master's Degree
COURSE INFORMATION General information relating to the current academic year Duration
2 years Program start
2009
Status Open Credits
120 CFU Type Master's degree courses Category Humanities and linguistics studies Regulations 270/04 Reform
Degree class LM-14 - Class of Master's degrees in Modern Philology Interuniversity Course No Open Admission Campus City Campus - Palazzo Prodi - via Tommaso Gar, 14
Department Department of Letters and Philosophy Verification of knowledge required for access No Program, texts and objectives Specific learning objectives of the course and description of the educational path Based on the knowledge and skills acquired during the first cycle of university studies, the Master's Degree Course in Philology and Literary Criticism aims to provide students with in-depth methodological, historical and critical training in linguistic, philological and literary disciplines from antiquity to the contemporary era. In particular, the course aims to provide the following skills:
- Ability to analyze any type of text in all its linguistic, philological, stylistic, historical-literary aspects;
- Ability to elaborate complex texts;
- Ability to organize and produce texts of a compilative nature;
- Textual and editing skills;
- Ability to use the main IT tools in specific areas of competence.
Graduates must therefore possess solid knowledge of literature and linguistics, with particular experience in the direct analysis of texts and documents. They must demonstrate full mastery of a language of the EU, in addition to Italian. The methods of delivery of the courses included in the educational path correspond to the types of lectures and seminars; lectures and seminars aim to develop students' critical and expository skills, both oral and written.
The educational path provides for the specialist study of themes relating to the cultural heritage transmitted from classical civilization to Italian literature, through the Romance world, within the framework of European literary civilization. Two typical career paths are proposed:
- Classical Philology: focused on the study of Greek and Latin language and literature;
- Modern Philology: focused on the linguistic and philological study of medieval and modern literatures with particular reference to Italian literature.
Knowledge required for access
1. Required knowledge for access Students who wish to enroll in the Master's Degree Course in Philology and Literary Criticism must have a suitable degree and must have obtained, during their previous studies, at least 60 CFU in the disciplines of the following SSDs: L-FIL-LET; L-ART; L-ANT; M-STO; L-LIN, M-GGR. In particular, the following must be obtained:
24 CFU in the following sectors: L-FIL-LET/10 Italian Literature, L-FIL-LET/11 Contemporary Italian Literature, L-FIL-LET/12 Italian Linguistics, L-FIL-LET/13 Philology of Italian Literature, L-FIL-LET/14 Literary Criticism and Comparative Literature.
Students who do not have the credits required for access may obtain them by enrolling in individual courses at the undergraduate level. Admission also requires IT skills and adequate mastery of at least one foreign language of the European Union.
2. Methods for verifying initial preparation A teaching committee, appointed annually within the teaching area, will verify the skills of the candidates through an interview. Graduates of the three-year degree course in Historical and Philological-Literary Studies (courses in Classical and Modern Literature) and those of the three-year degree course in Literature (509 curriculum) of the University of Trento are exempt from the interview.

COURSE: [0426H] HISTORICAL SCIENCES
TYPE: Master's Degree
SECTION: General description
URL: https://unitn.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10233

[0426H] HISTORICAL SCIENCES Master's Degree
Course currently under approval. INFO COURSES. General information for the current academic year. Duration: 2 years. Program: 2024. Status: Open. Credits: 120 CFU. Type: Master's degree courses. Category: Humanities and linguistics. Regulations: Reform 270/04. Degree class: LM-84 - Class of Master's degrees in Historical Sciences. Inter-university course: No. Open access: Yes. Location: City campus - Palazzo Prodi - via Tommaso Gar, 14. Department: Department of Letters and Philosophy. Verification of knowledge required for access: No. Program, texts and objectives. Admission procedure: There is no local scheduling of admissions for the inter-university Master's degree in Historical Sciences.
In order to be admitted to the Master's degree in Historical Sciences, students with a suitable degree must have obtained at least 60 CFU within the following scientific-disciplinary sectors: L-FIL-LET; M-STO; L-ANT; IUS; SECS-P; SECS-S/01,03,04,05; SPS; M-GGR/01,02; L-ART/01,02,03,04; M-DEA/01; M-FIL-06,08; M-PSI/05.
Of these 60 CFU, at least 24 must be obtained in the following sectors: L-ANT/02; L-ANT/03; M-STO/01,02,03,04,05,06,07,08,09.
The required knowledge must also include computer skills and knowledge of at least one foreign language of the EU, chosen from German, Spanish, French, or English, at a B1 level with computer skills.
The verification of the student's personal preparation in computer skills is usually carried out through certification or through an interview organized by the administrative office. Language skills are verified on the basis of certifications issued by the Language Centers of the two Universities or by bodies recognized by them.
In order to limit the need for candidates to travel, admission interviews can, upon request, also be held at the university that is not the administrative office for that session.

UNIVERSITY: University of Trento
COURSE: [0427G] MODERN LANGUAGES
TYPE: Bachelor's Degree
CURRICULUM: German, French, Spanish
SECTION: General Description
URL: https://unitn.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10438

[0427G] MODERN LANGUAGES Bachelor's Degree German, English, French, Italian, Spanish
COURSE INFORMATION General information relating to the current academic year Duration: 3 years. Admission year: 2014. Status: Open. Credits: 180 CFU. Type: Three-year Bachelor's degree. Category: Humanities and Linguistics. Regulations: Reform 270/04. Degree class: L-11 - Class of degrees in Modern Languages and Cultures. Inter-university course: No. Programmed access (226 places). Location: City campus - Palazzo Prodi - via Tommaso Gar, 14. Department: Department of Letters and Philosophy. Verification of knowledge required for access: No. Program, texts and objectives: Specific learning objectives of the course and description of the educational path. Graduates in "Modern Languages" will acquire:
- Level C1 in the four skills provided for by the Common European Framework of Reference in the two languages of study.
- The autonomy necessary to develop their skills and deepen their knowledge in a critical and methodologically correct way, of a legal-economic, historical-geographical, philological-literary and linguistic-cultural nature relating to the countries of the languages of study, also for the purpose of continuing the educational path in master's degrees and, subsequently, in doctoral studies.
- The ability to communicate and engage in international and intercultural dialogue, appropriately supported by clear presentation, effective argumentative skills, in both written and oral form, in the two languages of study and in Italian, as well as the possession of specific procedural skills.
- The ability to manage communication within organizations and companies that operate in the specific fields of languages and cultures in both a national and international context.
- Knowledge and understanding of linguistic-cultural differences and the ability to resolve conflicts, promote dialogue and cooperation, also with the aim of facilitating integration processes and new professional initiatives.
- The ability to analyze a written text through the knowledge of linguistic and communicative acts and functions and the use of tools of theoretical linguistics, contrastive analysis, as well as translation from and into the first/Italian language.
- A design capacity in the field of communication in various professional sectors (journalism, publishing, linguistic-cultural mediation, tourism, commercial offices and foreign relations).
Within the framework of these shared skills and abilities, students will also develop further specific skills:
- in the field of literary translation, through the formation of a solid theoretical foundation, knowledge of the main translation tools, including computer and multimedia tools, as well as the carrying out of internships in specific sectors;
- in the field of methodologies for the philological and literary analysis of texts relating to the cultures of the two foreign languages studied, with particular attention to linguistic phenomena and historical-literary events characteristic of those civilizations;
- in analytically and consciously addressing linguistic and cultural diversity;
- in using linguistic and interlinguistic mediation skills in a national and international context, with particular attention to aspects of intercultural and professional communication;
- in consciously using basic knowledge of an economic-business nature and business management with particular reference to marketing, strategy and organization and with applications in the tourism sector;
- in applying the basic knowledge of the geographical disciplines of territorial and environmental systems to develop a historical-artistic and landscape analysis of the territory, also with reference to tourist areas;
- in implementing skills related to territorial planning, design and resource management.
The educational path of the "Modern Languages" course is divided into two phases:
- a first phase of acquisition and consolidation of skills in the areas of theoretical linguistics and the Italian language (written and oral), languages and cultures of study, and the main computer and methodological tools;
- a second phase of acquisition of skills in communication, analysis and production of written and oral texts and in translation skills, both of a linguistic-literary nature aimed at deepening methodological tools relating to historical-cultural and philological-literary areas, including an introduction to literary translation, and relating to interlinguistic mediation in the areas of business and tourism.
Knowledge required for access: The provisions of Article 6, paragraph 1 of DM 22 OCTOBER 2004, n. 270 apply to admission and the knowledge required for access to the Degree Course in Modern Languages.
Adequate mastery of the Italian language and the use of basic computer tools are required for access to the Degree Course.
For details on the specific characteristics of the knowledge required and the related methods of verifying their possession, please refer to the didactic regulations of the Course, in which any additional training obligations may be indicated.

UNIVERSITY: University of Trento
COURSE: [0514G] COMPUTER SCIENCE
TYPE: Bachelor's Degree
SECTION: General Description
URL: https://unitn.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10114

[0514G] COMPUTER SCIENCE Bachelor's Degree
INFO TEACHING COURSES General information relating to the current academic year Duration
3 years Program start
2008
Status Open Credits
180 CFU Type Bachelor's degree Category Engineering, computer science and technology Regulations Reform 270/04
Degree class L-31 - Class of degrees in Computer Science and Technology Inter-university Course No. Limited Access (215 places)
Location Polo di collina - Povo B - via Sommarive, 9 (Sustainable capacity: 158)
Department Department of Engineering and Information Science Verification of knowledge required for access No. Program, texts and objectives Specific educational objectives of the course and description of the educational path. The Bachelor's Degree in Computer Science aims to train people who have solid foundations and a broad spectrum of skills in the field of science and information technology, a starting point both for directly accessing the world of work and for further study in master's degree, master's and doctoral programs. The Computer Science graduate is a professional with the ability to analyze complex systems (companies, services, natural and artificial systems), identify crucial processes in a systematic way, and propose models and solutions that can be implemented through software and computer systems. The mindset, given the emphasis on universal scientific tools and methods, is naturally predisposed to quickly integrate into different work contexts, adapting to the rapid innovation that characterizes the sector.
The degree course provides a common set of courses in which basic skills in mathematics and computer science are provided. Subsequently, two possible paths are distinguished:
- the Computer Science and Technology path, with two orientations, one of a methodological nature, intended for those who wish to continue their studies with a Master's Degree in Computer Science and Technology, and a professionalizing orientation, intended for those who wish to complete their studies at the end of the Bachelor's Degree in Computer Science.
- the Interdisciplinary path, intended for those who wish to complete their computer science training with other complementary disciplines in biology, economics and mathematics and continue their studies in other disciplines such as a Master's Degree in Medical Biotechnology, a Master's Degree in Finance, and a Master's Degree in Mathematical Sciences.
Knowledge required for access Students with a high school diploma or other equivalent qualification obtained abroad are admitted to the degree course. The necessary requirements to start studying regularly are the habit of reasoning rigorously, familiarity with mathematical language, and an interest in addressing and analyzing complex problems and in determining strategies and methods for their solution.
No detailed computer skills are required, nor is knowledge of the English language necessary, as the course offers all the opportunities to learn it. Even students with a humanistic background often demonstrate success in their studies and career that is absolutely comparable to that of students with an initial technical background.
The possibility of enrolling is conditional on passing a mandatory orientation and self-assessment test. The test adopted is the CISIA national test promoted and managed by the Interuniversity Consortium of Integrated Systems for Access (hereinafter CISIA), to which the University of Trento adheres, which is delivered both on an IT platform (CISIA Online Test) and in paper form.
The candidate who obtains a score of more than 20 for the paper version and 15 for the online version will be able to enroll without any outstanding requirements. For the paper version, the possibility of enrolling with outstanding requirements is provided for the candidate who obtains a score between 15 and 20. Further information is available on the website http://disi.unitn.it/test_ammissione_laurea_triennale_informatica. It is also possible to view practice tests on the website: http://www.cisiaonline.it/it/default/61/CISIA-practice-tests.html.

UNIVERSITY: University of Trento
COURSE: [0517H] COMPUTER SCIENCE
TYPE: Master's Degree
SECTION: General Description
URL: https://unitn.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10117

[0517H] COMPUTER SCIENCE Master's Degree - English
COURSE INFORMATION General information relating to the current academic year Duration: 2 years. Program: 2011. Status: Open. Credits: 120 CFU. Type of course: Master's degree. Category: Engineering, computer science and technology. Regulations: Reform 270/04. Degree class: LM-18 - Class of Master's Degrees in Computer Science. Inter-university course: No. Limited access (105 places). Location: Polo di collina - Povo B - via Sommarive, 9. Department: Department of Engineering and Information Science. Verification of knowledge required for access: No. Program, texts and objectives: Specific training objectives of the course and description of the training path. The Master's Degree in Computer Science aims to train individuals who possess both in-depth knowledge of the theoretical principles underlying computer science and specific skills in one of the macro-areas related to software technologies, systems and networks, bioinformatics, multimedia, dedicated systems, service engineering or security.
The course of study involves the acquisition of specialized computer knowledge, methodologies and technologies that enable graduates to plan, design, develop, manage, estimate, test and manage complex or innovative systems for the generation, transmission and processing of information.
During the training course, after acquiring the aforementioned knowledge and skills, students can choose to further consolidate their skills in a path on science and computer technologies, which provides advanced, innovative or experimental methodologies to address particularly complex technological problems at an industrial level, or possibly pursue a doctoral degree.
Alternatively, students can undertake interdisciplinary paths aimed at completing their computer training with economic/managerial knowledge on technological innovation and entrepreneurship in the ICT sector, or with interdisciplinary studies, or leading to a double degree with a foreign university.
Among the common objectives, we remember:
- the ability to work with broad autonomy, even assuming responsibility for large projects and structures.
- the deepening of the scientific method of investigation, the method of logical-deductive reasoning, and the inductive methods related to experimentation;
- knowledge of the fundamentals, techniques and methods of design and implementation of computer systems, both basic and application-oriented.
Among the specific objectives, we remember:
- the deepening of the scientific and technological aspects of one of the macro-areas of computer science or the expansion of one's skills in the study of more than one macro-area.
- the completion of one's training through training activities offered by other degree courses at the University of Trento (biology, economics, mathematics, etc.) or the activities related to entrepreneurship and innovation offered within the educational programs of the European Institute of Innovation and Technology.
Among the activities that specialist graduates will carry out, we indicate in particular: the analysis and creation of models for complex problems in various application contexts, the design and development of high-quality computer systems, the design of systems in areas related to computer science, in the sectors of research, industry, services, environment, health, cultural heritage and public administration. In addition, specific courses and seminars related to entrepreneurship will be offered, also with the participation of successful entrepreneurs, in order to promote full and satisfactory integration into the world of work and the creation of new companies.
The courses of the Master's Degree in Computer Science will be taught in English; this will allow, and already allows, to attract students from all over the world, and to establish double degree programs with the most prestigious European and American universities. This will allow students to immerse themselves in an international cultural environment that promotes both communication skills in English and intercultural relations.
Knowledge required for access: Students with a Bachelor's Degree or other equivalent degree obtained abroad are admitted to the Master's Degree Course, whose study curriculum includes, as a minimum requirement, basic computer knowledge and skills in programming, algorithms, data management and systems (hardware, operating systems, networks, etc.); as well as theoretical knowledge in the field of analysis and discrete mathematics. Advanced knowledge in the field of architectures, operating systems, algorithms, networks, theory of languages, in web systems may be fundamental depending on the path chosen in the Master's Degree. A knowledge of the English language at level B1 or higher is also required.
The didactic regulations describe in detail:
- the disciplinary and general knowledge and skills required for access to the Master's Degree Course;
- the methods in which the competent teaching structure verifies these knowledge and skills, specifying which Degree Courses can be considered "pass-through", i.e. allow automatic access to the Master's Degree, and which instead require an in-depth analysis of the curriculum and the student's motivations.
- the methods by which the competent teaching structure makes available to students and prospective students appropriate forms of self-assessment of these knowledge and skills.
Further details at the link: http://www.unitn.it/scienze/25364/requisiti-per-ammissione Admission procedures: The admission procedures to the Master's Degree Course in Computer Science are regulated within articles 4 and 5 of the didactic regulations of the course of study, which can be found online at: http://offertaformativa.unitn.it/it/lm/informatica/norme-e-regolamenti

URL: https://unitn.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10170

[0519H] MATHEMATICS Master's Degree - English
COURSE INFORMATION General information for the current academic year Duration: 2 years. Established: 2009. Status: Open. Credits: 120 CFU. Type: Master's degree courses. Category: Mathematics and Physics. Regulations: 270/04 reform. Degree class: LM-40 - Class of Master's Degrees in Mathematics. Inter-university course: No. Open access: Yes. Location: Polo di collina - Povo A - via Sommarive, 14 (Sustainable capacity: 47). Department: Department of Mathematics. Verification of required knowledge for access: No. Program, texts and objectives: Specific training objectives of the course and description of the educational path. The course aims to train graduates who have a solid knowledge of various areas of mathematics (algebra, analysis, geometry, probability, numerical analysis, mathematical physics), and a more in-depth knowledge in at least one specific area. This knowledge is usually accompanied by an operational familiarity with at least one other discipline.
These skills, in addition to being based on solid theoretical foundations, also include the ability to:
- solve non-routine mathematical problems, knowing how to draw on their own cultural background to use the most appropriate tools;
- carry out complex bibliographic research, in order to be able to identify useful tools in the literature, even recent ones, for solving given problems;
- present mathematical topics effectively, also in English, adapting the presentation to different types of audiences, even non-specialized ones.
Through the thesis, the student is finally guided to move autonomously and creatively in a specific area.
The entire Master's Degree course is taught in English.
The didactic regulations of the Master's Degree course provide for paths aimed more at a broad mathematical cultural training and paths intended to allow the graduate to understand the language, methods, and characteristics of a non-mathematical discipline, together with its relationship with mathematics itself. With this training, the graduate will be able to work more effectively in multidisciplinary working groups, making a significant contribution to modeling and solving complex problems.
Required knowledge for access: To be admitted to the Master's Degree course in Mathematics, applicants must hold a degree or university diploma of at least three years' duration, or another qualification obtained abroad and recognized as equivalent, and specific curricular requirements which will be indicated in the didactic regulations of the Master's Degree course.
The student's personal preparation will also be verified, using the methods that will be detailed in the didactic regulations of the master's degree course.
Taking into account the specificities of the initial preparation, according to the methods provided for in the didactic regulations of the master's degree course, admission may be subject to the student choosing a study plan, agreed with the Course Council, which must in any case comply with the didactic regulations.
This last possibility is particularly aimed at allowing and regulating access to the Master's Degree course also for graduates (three-year degrees) in another discipline, who intend to follow an interdisciplinary path between this discipline and Mathematics, and who in the previous academic career have obtained a certain number of credits in Mathematics. Typically, these students will be asked to dedicate a substantial amount of credits to Mathematics, while the free credits chosen by the student will obviously not be restricted.
A knowledge of the English language equal to level B1 or higher will be required.

UNIVERSITY: University of Trento
COURSE: [0520H] CELLULAR AND MOLECULAR BIOTECHNOLOGIES
TYPE: Master's Degree
SECTION: General description
URL: https://unitn.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10232

[0520H] CELLULAR AND MOLECULAR BIOTECHNOLOGIES Master's Degree - English
COURSE INFORMATION General information relating to the current academic year Duration
2 years Program start
2012
Status Open Credits
120 CFU Course type Master's degree courses Category Medical, health and biological sciences Regulations Reform 270/04
Degree class LM-9 - Class of Master's Degrees in Medical, Veterinary and Pharmaceutical Biotechnology Interuniversity Course No Programmed Access (60 places)
Location Hill Campus - Povo B - via Sommarive, 9 (Sustainable capacity: 60)
Department Department of Cellular, Computational and Integrated Biology - CIBIO Verification of knowledge required for access No Program, texts and objectives Specific learning objectives of the course and description of the educational path Master's graduates in Cellular and Molecular Biotechnology are experts with high levels of competence to design and conduct analysis and research activities, technological and experimental research, research and development in Cellular and Molecular Biotechnology, with particular reference to the biomedical field.
The course aims to prepare graduates who have advanced skills in the development of scientific methodologies, who are able to coordinate study and research projects at national and international level and who possess in-depth specialist knowledge in the fields of biotechnology applied to areas of interest in biomedicine. In particular, the course explores the themes of the genetic and molecular basis of diseases and the pathophysiology of the human organism, providing the knowledge necessary to develop diagnostic and prognostic pathways, as well as therapeutic strategies based on the use of biotechnology. This knowledge is integrated with solid biophysical, technological, statistical and computer training, which allows the graduate in Cellular and Molecular Biotechnology to carry out, on a genomic basis, preclinical experiments on the molecular pathogenesis of human diseases and on the definition of new therapeutic approaches.
The educational path allows the student to acquire:
-in-depth knowledge of the mechanisms of functioning and regulation of eukaryotic cells, in physiological and pathological conditions;
-in-depth knowledge of human pathological processes, at the molecular, cellular and systemic level;
-skills in the methodologies relating to the techniques of culture and genetic engineering of eukaryotic cells;
-in-depth knowledge of the physical methods aimed at the structural and functional characterization of biological macromolecules and the cellular processes in which they are involved;
-skills in bioinformatics methodologies for the organization and access to databases, with particular reference to genomics, proteomics and metabolomics;
-knowledge and skills in molecular modeling for the design of molecules of interest in biomedicine;
-knowledge for the design and industrial production of biotechnological products, such as biopharmaceuticals, diagnostics, vaccines;
-basic knowledge relating to the economy, organization and management of companies and the management of innovation and biotechnological development projects, including aspects relating to intellectual property.
The acquisition of further knowledge is also planned through the student's autonomous choice of training activities among those suggested by the course of study or among those offered by the University to complete the preparation in areas related to Cellular and Molecular Biotechnology.
The educational path is completed with a phase of experimental research activities, aimed at preparing the final exam, in specialized academic laboratories, in companies, public administration structures and laboratories, as well as in other Italian and European universities, also within the framework of international agreements.
Knowledge required for access Those who have a degree obtained in class L-2 (Biotechnology) of DM 270/04 or in class 1 (Biotechnology) of the previous DM 509/99 can access the Master's Degree course in Cellular and Molecular Biotechnology. Those who have a degree of at least three years or an equivalent title deemed suitable according to current regulations, or another qualification obtained abroad and recognized as suitable, can also access, provided that they have acquired the basic curricular requirements as defined according to the methods established in the Didactic Regulations of the Master's Degree Course.
Considering that the language in which the Master's Degree Course in Cellular and Molecular Biotechnology will be held is English, knowledge of the English language at a level adequate for understanding the lessons is mandatory, as defined by the Didactic Regulations. The Didactic Regulations of the course of study will also define the timing and methods for verifying the students' personal preparation.

UNIVERSITY: University of Trento
COURSE: [0522H] DATA SCIENCE
TYPE: Master's Degree
SECTION: General Description
URL: https://unitn.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10692

[0522H] DATA SCIENCE Master's Degree - English
INFO General information regarding the current academic year. Duration: 2 years. Admission: 2018. Status: Open. Credits: 120 CFU. Type: Master's Degree Courses. Category: Engineering, Computer Science and Technology. Regulations: Reform 270/04. Degree Class: LM-91 - Master's Degree in Techniques and Methods for Information Society. Inter-University Course: No. Open Admission: Yes. Department: Interdepartmental Center for Mind/Brain - CIMeC, Department of Economics and Management, Department of Industrial Engineering, Department of Information Engineering and Science, Department of Mathematics, Department of Psychology and Cognitive Science, Department of Sociology and Social Research (Main).
Verification of knowledge required for admission: No. Program, texts and objectives: Specific learning objectives of the course and description of the educational path. The Master's Degree in Data Science trains professionals with strong transversal skills who can work in multidisciplinary environments. Graduates in Data Science will be able to manage and analyze large amounts of data produced by natural and social systems to support decision-making in economic-productive, political-social fields, and in scientific research activities in the public administration, industry, public and private services, and the third sector.
Graduates in Data Science will be able to hold high-level technical and/or managerial roles in contexts that require a good combination of skills in computer science, mathematics, statistics, and social, psychological, and economic sciences.
At the end of the program, graduates in Data Science will be able to analyze the elements that contribute to the formation of the data under study and identify possible sources of noise, distortion, and uncertainty; they will be able to use computer platforms for data storage, management, and transformation, being aware of the performance limitations and/or advantages offered by the various platforms; they will be able to identify the strategic objectives that can be best pursued through data analysis, effectively combining the methodologies of social, psychological, and business management and public and private administration with the technologies and methodologies of computer science, possessing skills in each of the areas.
In more detail, graduates in Data Science:
- are able to understand the origin and characteristics of the data processed; they know the ICT technologies related to the life cycle of data and their performance limitations; they know how to analyze and manage the flow of data generation, acquisition, transmission, and access; they know how to manage and integrate heterogeneous archives of statistical and administrative data;
- are able to combine the methods and techniques of social and psychological sciences, business management, and public and private administration with the technologies and methodologies of computer science and data analysis of mathematics and statistics, possessing skills in each of the areas and being able to effectively interpret technological and organizational change in companies and administrations;
- are able to analyze and interpret data according to its nature and variety, applying the most appropriate analytical approach to respond to the activities or objectives of the organization or public or private entity;
- are able to identify and access data sources and choose the most suitable and effective methods and models to support and guide decision-making processes and strategic choices of companies and management, they know how to develop lines of evolution, operational plans, and generate indications and programs for the development of action, also through the application of techniques for reducing dimensional complexity and the development of predictive models to generate advanced knowledge systems;
- are able to operate in interdisciplinary working groups and know how to use the most appropriate communication and storytelling methods to present empirical evidence in the most suitable form to support the tactical and strategic decisions of management, paying particular attention to the issues related to the synthesis and effective representation and visualization of information; they are able to use fluently, in written and oral form, English as well as Italian, with reference also to disciplinary lexicons.
- have basic legal knowledge in the areas and issues related to the use of information technologies and data processing (with reference, among others, to issues of security, privacy protection, legal validity).
The interdepartmental nature of the study program allows to welcome students from different backgrounds, providing them with a highly interdisciplinary curriculum.
The first courses will have programs aimed at integrating the different skills and will cover the fundamental disciplines of Computer Science, Mathematics, Statistics, and Social, Psychological, and Economic Sciences. These will be followed by courses and laboratories on relevant applications of Data Science, in particular for Social, Psychological, and Economic Sciences. A suitable offer of optional courses and laboratories will allow the design of paths aimed at specific areas. Finally, graduates in Data Science will have a basic cultural, scientific, and methodological preparation that will allow them to access subsequent levels of university study (second-level master's degrees and doctorates).
Knowledge required for admission: Admission to the master's degree program will be subject to verification of the following requirements, the possession of which will be a necessary condition for enrollment:
a) curricular requirements:
- possession of a first-level degree obtained at Italian Universities or another degree obtained abroad, deemed suitable;
- a linguistic competence of at least level B2 in English;
- possession of at least 6 CFU related to courses belonging to the SSD Computer Science (INF*/ING-INF*), 6 CFU related to courses belonging to the SSD of Human, Social, Legal and Economic disciplines (SPS*/SECS-P*/M-PSI*/IUS*), 6 CFU related to courses belonging to the SSD of technical-scientific disciplines of Mathematics (MAT*) and Statistics (SECS-S*) and at least another 24 CFU in total in these SSDs.
b) specific preparation requirements:
- demonstrate good analytical skills in social science and information science topics,
- demonstrate good skills in addressing methodological problems,
- demonstrate good skills in reading research data.
The assessment of personal preparation is carried out according to the criteria and methods defined by the didactic regulations of the study program.
The didactic regulations of the study program will also define in detail:
- the methods for verifying knowledge of the English language, as well as the times and methods for verifying the personal preparation of students;
- the methods by which the competent teaching structure verifies the knowledge and skills required.

-----

UNIVERSITY: University of Trento
COURSE: [0611G] SOCIOLOGY
TYPE: Bachelor's Degree
SECTION: General Description
URL: https://unitn.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10137

[0611G] SOCIOLOGY Bachelor's Degree
COURSE INFORMATION General information relating to the current academic year Duration: 3 years Admission: 2023 Status: Open Credits: 180 CFU Type: Three-year Bachelor's Degree Category: Sociology and International Studies Regulations: Reform 270/04 Degree Class: L-40 - Class of Sociology Degrees Inter-University Course: No Programmed Access (150 Places) Location: City Campus - Sociology - via Verdi, 26 Department: Department of Sociology and Social Research Verification of knowledge required for access: No Program, texts and objectives: Specific learning objectives of the course and description of the educational path. The Sociology degree program provides students with the necessary tools, in terms of skills and understanding, to carry out analysis, coordination and management, design, communication, organization and evaluation functions in public and private professional contexts, which constitute the natural occupational destinations for the professional profile graduating from the degree program.
Students receive a basic sociological education enriched by the contribution of the main disciplines of the humanities and social sciences (anthropology, political science, history, psychology, economics, law, statistics), training in the methods and techniques of the main empirical research approaches, and more in-depth training in some special sociologies. The underlying objective of the degree program is to offer students, through a gradual and linear educational path, a series of tools that enable them to conduct analyses and interpretations of social reality.
During the three-year period, students are presented with the concepts and theories of classical and contemporary sociological theory, with a strong focus on their applicability in an analytical key. At the same time, students in the degree program learn the methods and techniques of social research and statistics, putting the knowledge acquired into practice through concrete research experiences. In parallel, the comparison with the teachings of anthropology, social psychology, political science, economics and law allows them to learn the conceptual vocabulary of the broader world of social sciences, enriching the sociological perspective and integrating it with essential tools, in terms of understanding and skills, to be able to operate in an expert and competent manner in work contexts.
After a solid common basic education, the study plan develops according to the path chosen as most appropriate by the students. This allows the development of specific skills in graduates necessary to operate effectively in one of the following professional areas: promotion of community development and collective action with a view to environmental and social sustainability; management of organizational innovation and promotion of social entrepreneurship; conducting socio-economic and demographic descriptive and explanatory research, promotion and intervention in the field of cultural production and communication.
The following is a general analysis of the learning areas:
1. Sociological Disciplines: include those courses aimed at both the study of the main sociological theories and the in-depth study of their related methods and techniques of empirical investigation, and also include courses relating to specific sociologies, e.g. economic and labor sociology, sociology of science, sociology of culture, sociology of inequalities and diversity.
2. Economic-statistical disciplines: include those disciplines through which the student acquires the fundamentals of economics and the elements of social statistics.
3. Legal-political disciplines: include those disciplines through which the student acquires the basic elements of law and political science.
4. Anthropological, historical and psychological disciplines: include those courses aimed at the study of cultural anthropology, social psychology and history.
Knowledge required for access: In addition to a high school diploma or other qualification obtained abroad and recognized as equivalent, students must pass an entrance test consisting of questions designed to verify:
- comprehension of the text and knowledge of the Italian language;
- knowledge and skills acquired in their studies;
- logical reasoning.
If the verification of the knowledge required for access is not positive, specific additional training obligations will be indicated to be fulfilled in the first year of the course. The course may be subject to programmed enrollment.

UNIVERSITY: University of Trento
COURSE: [0620G] INTERNATIONAL STUDIES
TYPE: Bachelor's Degree
SECTION: General Description
URL: https://unitn.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10565

[0620G] INTERNATIONAL STUDIES Bachelor's Degree
COURSE INFORMATION General information relating to the current academic year. Duration: 3 years. Program: 2015. Status: Open. Credits: 180 ECTS. Type: Three-year Bachelor's Degree. Category: Sociology and International Studies. Regulations: Reform 270/04. Degree Class: L-36 - Class of Degrees in Political Science and International Relations. Inter-University Course: No. Limited Access (20 places). Location: City Campus - Sociology - via Verdi, 26. Department: Department of Sociology and Social Research. Verification of knowledge required for access: No. Program, texts and objectives: Specific learning objectives of the course and description of the educational path. The main educational objective of the course is to enable students to understand the complexity of the phenomena that affect contemporary societies, studying them from different disciplinary perspectives. The perspective used is that of the transformations induced by globalization, internationalization, and Europeanization on national social, political, and economic systems.
Since its inception, the Bachelor's Degree in International Studies has been based on the awareness that a full understanding of these phenomena can only come from a multidisciplinary and interdisciplinary approach. The processes of globalization and internationalization of national societies raise new questions for the social sciences, making the need for interconnection and dialogue between economic, legal, political, sociological, and historical disciplines increasingly evident. This approach is reinforced and enhanced in the new curriculum.
The Bachelor's Degree course can be structured into paths that differ in the professional and educational profiles that are intended to be pursued. Although sharing a multidisciplinary approach, the courses offered in the two paths can address themes that are both complementary and distinct.
The Bachelor's Degree course is structured over three years. During the first year, students must learn the "fundamentals" of the main social sciences and statistics. In the field of sociology, the main concepts and notions necessary for the analysis of the configuration of society and social change are introduced. In the field of modern and contemporary history, the main events and developments of the modern and contemporary world and their most important interpretations are presented. In the field of political science, the language and conceptualizations specific to the discipline and the analysis of the democratic process are introduced. In the field of economics, particular attention is paid to the main macroeconomic theories. In the field of public law, elements of European public law are introduced, understood as the combination of national and supranational public law. In the field of statistics, the first rudiments relating to data analysis are presented. Great importance is also given to knowledge of the English language.
During the second year, the essential tools of empirical research in the field of social sciences and knowledge of international law are presented. The political systems are also presented in a comparative key, as well as their behavior and their relations within the international system. In the historical field, the history of the modern state is analyzed, as a historically determined form of organization of power, in the European-Western context, from its medieval origins to the consolidation of the modern age, ending with some references to the crisis of the contemporary state in the face of globalization processes. As in the first year, a specialist English course completes the language offering. Some courses may be specific to the educational paths.
Finally, during the third year, courses are offered that focus on more specific issues. These are dedicated to the main research topics in the field of European and international studies, the issues of social inequalities, social movements and European civil society, the issues of citizenship and European democracy, conflict management and others. A course in international economic policy completes the multidisciplinary training. By the end of the third year, students must also demonstrate knowledge of a second foreign language. To complete the educational path, some credit seminars are activated on particularly relevant topics concerning the transformation of contemporary economic, political and social systems.
Knowledge required for access: Before accessing the Bachelor's Degree course, students must possess the following skills:
a) analytical skills:
among the different types of analytical skills, the following are considered particularly important: the ability to reason logically; for example, they must be able to understand structured sets of relationships, to deduce new information from them, to analyze and evaluate arguments, to draw precise and accurate inferences, and to identify possible causal explanations, etc.
b) verbal skills:
among the various types of verbal skills, the course considers the following two to be most effective in determining the quality of the study path:
- ability in understanding texts: for example, they must be able to isolate the various passages in which a fact is presented, exemplified, developed; to establish logical connections between the various passages; to synthesize the information provided by the text; etc.
- ability in lexical understanding: for example, they must be able to identify analogies between terms in the lexicon, to identify the opposite of a given term, etc.
c) cognitive skills:
the knowledge required to access the Bachelor's Degree course is as follows:
- a keen interest in subjects related to European studies, international studies, and development cooperation;
- knowledge of the main computer programs for writing texts and browsing the internet (e.g., Word and Internet Explorer);
- knowledge (at a minimum A2 level) of a community language, usually English, in addition to Italian.
The verification of the adequacy of the candidates' personal preparation can be carried out through entrance tests.
The didactic regulations of the Bachelor's Degree course define any additional training obligations to be achieved in the event of failure to pass the verification of the knowledge required for access.
