UNIVERSITY: University of Trento
COURSE: [0623H] GLOBAL AND LOCAL STUDIES
TYPE: Master's Degree
SECTION: General Description
URL: https://unitn.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10733

[0623H] GLOBAL AND LOCAL STUDIES Master's Degree - Italian/English
COURSE INFORMATION General information relating to the current academic year Duration: 2 years. Program start: 2020. Status: Open. Credits: 120 ECTS. Course Type: Master's Degree. Category: Sociology and International Studies. Regulations: Reform 270/04. Degree Class: LM-62 - Master's Degree in Political Science. Inter-university Course: No. Open Admission: Yes. Location: City Campus - Sociology - via Verdi, 26. Department: Department of Sociology and Social Research. Verification of knowledge required for access: No. Program, texts and objectives: Specific learning objectives of the course and description of the educational path. The Global and Local Studies (Glocal Studies) degree program aims to train professionals capable of designing and guiding public policies and development interventions by combining the analysis of the specific characteristics of the local contexts to which they are addressed with the analysis of the global processes that affect them. Graduates will be able to analyze the local context in a rigorous manner in light of the changes induced by global dynamics, manage participatory processes aimed at producing public policy decisions, interact with multi-level decision-making processes and institutions, and provide local actors with the complex set of knowledge relating to development opportunities. More specifically, the graduate will be a professional: a) an expert and consultant in public policies operating in the context of local, national and international organizations, whether governmental or non-governmental; b) a consultant for territorial projects, with analytical skills applicable in the design and implementation of development and governance interventions in local contexts. In general, the degree program prepares students to analyze and intervene in specific territorial areas and on different scales, with an understanding of the global processes that affect them. The term "Glocal," which has been adopted and consolidated in the language of social, political and territorial studies for several decades, refers to those processes that intersect, concretize and locally characterize the dynamics of globalization, and as such constitute a particular object of study for the degree program. The degree program is characterized by a strongly multidisciplinary approach and is based on political, sociological, historical, economic and legal-institutional studies. During the first year, students will acquire: - skills in quantitative and qualitative research methodology that will enable them to conduct empirical research, whether based on primary or secondary data. In detail, students will learn methods and techniques for collecting, processing and analyzing both statistical data - from drafting a questionnaire to coding the results - and ethnographic data, from conducting interviews and focus groups to observation, relating to the areas of study addressed in the degree program; - factual knowledge relating to the social, political, economic and historical processes inherent in globalization processes, which will enable them to specify and document the multi-level challenges that affect local contexts; - the ability to define an area of specialization in studies among several possible thematic areas. The degree program's educational offering, in fact, explores and deepens heterogeneous glocalization processes relating to environmental and migratory issues, urban and territorial issues, national and European policies, development cooperation, conflict and democratization; - skills in the design and management of research, action research and policies, in order to train professionals capable of following the empirical implementation of the knowledge acquired in each of its phases, from design to planning to public dissemination of the surveys and interventions implemented in the context of development and governance interventions in local contexts; - language skills, with particular reference to the English language. The Master's degree program offers compulsory courses in both Italian and English, which, through participatory teaching, allow students to exercise their written and oral expression skills in multiple languages. In the second year of the course, the analysis of public policies or the theories of local and territorial development will be deepened, depending on the chosen path. Students can also choose to deepen their knowledge in various areas concerning the environment, migration dynamics, ethnic relations, geography and demography, the transformations of democracy, and political culture. These courses are particularly important in order to acquire theoretical and empirical knowledge on socio-political dynamics that characterize specific areas of activity, such as environmental sustainability or the reception of refugees and immigrants. Through the educational activities and internship opportunities offered by the degree program, graduates will have acquired a series of soft skills, including: - the ability to work in multidisciplinary contexts, cooperating with people with different disciplinary backgrounds; - communication skills, such as writing and presenting a policy brief and/or a project outline in a clear and effective manner; - the ability to search for information from online and offline sources, discerning reliability and accuracy; - the ability to work in a group. Further professional skills are acquired through in-depth seminars specifically organized for students of the degree program. Knowledge required for access: Access to the degree program is subject to the possession of the following curricular requirements: 1) a degree or university diploma obtained after completing a course of study lasting at least three years in the following degree classes: L36 (Political Science and International Relations), L37 (Social Sciences for Cooperation, Development and Peace), L39 (Social Work), L40 (Sociology), or another degree obtained abroad and recognized as equivalent; or: 1) a degree or university diploma obtained after completing a course of study lasting at least three years or another degree obtained abroad and recognized as equivalent 2) possession of at least 36 ECTS in at least one of the SPS/04, SPS/07, SECS-P/02, IUS/09, M-STO/04 sectors 3) possession of at least 12 ECTS in at least one of the SECS-S/04, SPS/01, SPS/08, SPS/09, SPS/10, SPS/11, M-DEA/01, M-GGR/02 sectors. In addition to these, there are the requirements relating to the student's personal preparation: 1) knowledge of the English language at least at level B2 (Common European Framework of Reference). 2) adequate personal preparation, which will be verified through the assessment of the first-level degree according to the procedures specified in the didactic regulations of the degree program. In the case of a foreign student, access to the degree program is subject to the possession of all the aforementioned requirements and, in addition: 3) knowledge of the Italian language at least at level B2 (Common European Framework of Reference). The student must possess the curricular requirements before the verification of personal preparation; in particular, the assignment of additional educational debts or obligations is not allowed.

UNIVERSITY: University of Trento

[0625H] DATA SCIENCE Master's Degree - English
COURSE INFORMATION General information relating to the current academic year Duration: 2 years Admission: 2024 Approval Date: 15-2-2024 Status: Open Credits: 120 CFU Type: Master's Degree Course Category: Engineering, Computer Science and Technology Regulations: Reform 270/04 Degree Class: LM Data - Data Science Inter-University Course: No Programmed Access (56 places) Location: City Campus - Sociology - via Verdi, 26 Department: Interdepartmental Center for Mind/Brain - CIMeC Department of Economics and Management Department of Industrial Engineering Department of Information Engineering and Science Department of Mathematics Department of Psychology and Cognitive Sciences Department of Sociology and Social Research (Main) Verification of knowledge required for access: No Program, texts and objectives: Specific learning objectives of the course and description of the educational path. The Master's Degree in Data Science trains professionals with strong transversal skills capable of: 1) managing and analyzing large volumes of data produced by natural and social systems to support decision-making processes in economic-productive, political-social contexts and in scientific research activities in the public administration, industry, public and private services and the third sector; 2) holding high-level technical and/or managerial roles in contexts that require a good combination of skills in computer science, mathematics, statistics and social, psychological and economic sciences.
More specifically, graduates in Data Science are able to:
- identify, represent, acquire, manage and analyze large quantities of heterogeneous data in the most appropriate way and using state-of-the-art techniques to make a decisive contribution to achieving the objectives of the organization/body with which they collaborate;
- interpret and/or anticipate the needs of the sector in which they operate and propose effective solutions, thereby supporting decision-making processes and strategic choices of the company and management, and contributing to the development of evolution lines, operational plans and the generation of indications and programs for the development of the actions of the organization/body with which they collaborate;
- recognize and exploit the specific characteristics as well as manage the problems of the infrastructures underlying Data Science, both with regard to the generation and processing of large quantities of structured and unstructured data;
- operate in multiple contexts, even highly interdisciplinary, as they possess systematic computer and mathematical-statistical skills as well as fundamental knowledge of the legal, ethical, business and/or social aspects related to the acquisition, management, processing and use of data;
- communicate the results of their work effectively, even through new technologies, techniques and visualization tools.
These objectives are achieved during a path structured in:
1) a first year of alignment which includes: a) an activity of integration of the skills already possessed by the enrolled students through the differentiated attendance, depending on the first-level degrees obtained, of a series of courses aimed at strengthening knowledge and skills in the disciplines of information science, social, economic and psychological-cognitive sciences; b) a specific training activity in Data Science through a series of courses aimed at all enrolled students, which are designed to provide specific skills in the disciplines of computer science and mathematical-statistical sciences fundamental to working in this field;
2) a second year of specialization of the skills of the enrolled students in different areas of application of Data Science. The training activity provides for a single exit path that can be characterized flexibly by combining courses aimed at providing specific domain knowledge to the areas of application of Data Science (social, economic, psychological, mathematical, computer science) with laboratory courses aimed at providing advanced skills in the collection, analysis and interpretation of large amounts of data and high-level and cutting-edge technical skills in the field of Data Science. The second year's training offer is completed by the possibility of including in the study plan free choice credits obtained by successfully passing any course offered by the Master's Degree as well as by the university academic structures, as long as they are consistent with the training objectives of the Course.
As this is a Master's Degree course in English, training activities are provided for students whose native language is not Italian, aimed at acquiring the knowledge of Italian required for the graduate.
Knowledge required for access: To access the Master's Degree, a Bachelor's degree or university diploma of at least three years' duration, or another qualification obtained abroad and recognized as equivalent, and the possession of specific curricular requirements are required. Adequate personal preparation is also required, which must be verified.
In any case, the assignment of additional educational debts or obligations is not allowed.
Access to the Master's Degree Course is subject to the verification of the following requirements, the possession of which will be a necessary condition for enrollment:
a) curricular requirements:
i. having obtained a first-level degree at Italian Universities or another qualification obtained abroad recognized as equivalent according to current regulations;
ii. having at least:
- 6 CFU in the SSDs INF* and/or ING-INF*
- 6 CFU in the SSDs SPS* and/or SECS-P* and/or M-PSI*/IUS*
- 6 CFU in the SSDs MAT* and/or SECS-S*
- further 24 CFU in total in the SSDs listed above. The possession of the fundamental knowledge of computer science and probability and statistics will be verified through the evaluation of the syllabi of the exams taken and related to the disciplines in question.
b) having a certificate of English language proficiency of at least level B2.
The possession of the personal preparation of the candidates as well as the knowledge of the English language are verified according to the methods indicated in the didactic regulations of the course.

UNIVERSITY: University of Trento
COURSE: [0707H] PSYCHOLOGY
TYPE: Master's Degree
SECTION: General Description
URL: https://unitn.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10124

[0707H] PSYCHOLOGY Master's Degree
COURSE INFORMATION General information relating to the current academic year Duration: 2 years. Admission year: 2023. Status: Open. Credits: 120 ECTS. Type: Master's degree courses. Category: Psychology and Neuroscience. Regulations: Reform 270/04. Degree class: LM-51 - Class of Master's Degrees in Psychology. Inter-university course: No. Programmed access (122 places). Location: Rovereto Campus - Palazzo Piomarta - corso Bettini, 84. Department: Department of Psychology and Cognitive Sciences. Verification of knowledge required for access: No. Program, texts and objectives: Specific educational objectives of the course and description of the educational path. The graduate who obtains the Master's degree in Psychology will have acquired in-depth knowledge in the psychological field, based on scientifically valid models, as well as the operational skills necessary for the practice of psychological professions. In particular, the objective of the course is to enable students to acquire the theoretical knowledge and advanced methodological skills in the field of psychological disciplines necessary for application in the various contexts of professional action in psychology. The educational path is based on a solid preparation in the various disciplinary areas, ranging from clinical psychology to neuropsychology, to social and organizational psychology, with particular attention to the neurobiological basis of mental phenomena, to methodological and research aspects, and to psychological and psychosocial intervention.
The student will consolidate their specialized knowledge of cognitive, affective and social processes, their biological basis and their development, as well as operational skills on the most advanced study methods of these functions and on the main techniques of habilitation and rehabilitation. The student will also acquire the most advanced methodological skills in the diagnostic field to promote the understanding of the different intervention models.
The educational objectives of the course are achieved through the articulation of the educational offer in educational paths that share some educational activities in the disciplinary areas that characterize Psychology but differ during the two-year period, allowing for the deepening of specific topics. This will allow the student to deepen specific areas, such as, for example, that of Neuroscience, which focuses on mental mechanisms and the neurobiological basis of cognitive and affective functions, their disorders and their rehabilitation; that of Clinical Psychology, focused on topics related to the main explanatory models of discomfort and mental disorders in the life cycle, as well as their treatment, in a multi-approach and integrated with cognitive neuroscience; that of Human Resources and Organizational Psychology, which allows to acquire the knowledge and skills necessary to work in complex organizational contexts and to manage human resources effectively. The educational paths will be defined in detail in the educational regulations of the course.
The student will be involved in frontal and seminar teaching activities, with ample space dedicated to participatory teaching activities, which offers the most appropriate context for discussions and personal reflections on the topics of study and for the acquisition of professionalizing work tools and methods. The training is also aimed at acquiring advanced knowledge of the English language, including the specialist vocabulary used in psychology, necessary for continuous updating of professional skills. Students are also offered the opportunity to attend training activities abroad as part of the Erasmus program and the numerous collaborations with foreign universities.
Pursuant to Articles 1 and 3 of Law No. 163 of November 8, 2021, the final examination for the award of the Master's degree in Psychology - class LM-51, qualifies for the practice of the profession of psychologist. To this end, the aforementioned final examination includes the carrying out of a practical evaluation test (PPV) of the professional skills acquired with the internship within the courses of study, aimed at ascertaining the level of technical preparation of the candidate for the qualification to practice the profession, which precedes the discussion of the degree thesis. In order to pass the PPV, the student must obtain a positive assessment, following which he/she will be admitted to the discussion of the degree thesis. Those who obtain a positive assessment of the Practical-Evaluative Internship (PEI) within the courses of study are admitted to the final examination. Within the professionalizing educational activities, 20 university educational credits (CFU) are acquired through the carrying out of a PEI, within the courses of study. The PEI consists of contextualized and supervised practical activities, which provide for direct observation and the carrying out of activities aimed at situated learning and the development of the procedural and relational skills essential for the exercise of the professional activity. These skills refer to the typical and reserved acts that characterize the profession of psychologist, also in accordance with Article 1 of Law No. 56 of February 18, 1989, and include the use of the tools for knowledge and intervention for the prevention, diagnosis, habilitation-rehabilitation activities, psychological support aimed at the person, the group, social organizations and communities, as well as experimental, research and teaching activities. The PEI is passed by obtaining a final positive assessment. In order to obtain the 30 ECTS of PEI, part of the professionalizing educational activities, corresponding to 10 ECTS, is carried out according to the methods provided for by the Interministerial Decree No. 654 of 5-7-2022 and subsequent amendments and additions.
Knowledge required for access: Those who possess the following curricular requirements may be admitted to the course:
- a degree or a three-year university diploma, or other qualification obtained abroad and recognized as suitable;
- psychological skills certified by the possession of at least 88 ECTS distributed in several disciplinary areas of psychology (M-PSI/01, M-PSI/02, M-PSI/03, M-PSI/04, M-PSI/05, M-PSI/06, M-PSI/07, M-PSI/08) as specified in the educational regulations of the course;
- the ECTS of practical-evaluative internship provided for by the Interministerial Decree No. 654/2022 and subsequent amendments and additions. In the absence, in whole or in part, of the recognition of the ECTS referred to in paragraph 6, three-year graduates acquire the missing ECTS of the internship in addition to the 120 ECTS of the Master's degree.
In addition to these, there are the requirements relating to the student's personal preparation:
- knowledge of the English language at least at level B1 (Common European Framework of Reference);
- adequate personal preparation which will be verified by means of a test with questions relating to the various areas of psychology, according to the methods laid down in the educational regulations of the course.
The preparation of the test and the evaluation of the results will be monitored by a special commission, according to the methods laid down in the educational regulations of the course.
The student must possess the curricular requirements before the verification of the personal preparation; in particular, the assignment of additional educational debts or obligations is not allowed.

UNIVERSITY: University of Trento
COURSE: [INT - ISMP] ENGINEERING OF MEDICAL SYSTEMS FOR THE PERSON
TYPE: Bachelor's Degree
CURRICULUM: Inter-university
SECTION: General Description
URL: https://unitn.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10799

[INT - ISMP] ENGINEERING OF MEDICAL SYSTEMS FOR THE PERSON Bachelor's Degree Inter-university Italian English
Course currently under approval INFO General information relating to the current academic year Duration
3 years Program
2024
Status Open Credits
180 CFU Type Bachelor's degree Category Engineering, computer science and technology Regulations Reform 270/04
Degree class L-8 R - Information Engineering Inter-university Course Participating Universities University of Verona Access Open Admission University of Verona Department Department of Industrial Engineering Verification of knowledge required for access No Program, texts and objectives Specific learning objectives of the course and description of the educational path The Bachelor's Degree in Computer, Communication and Electronic Engineering aims to train professionals with general skills in the field of information engineering, and, depending on the student's chosen specialization, specific skills in the areas of telecommunications, electronics and computer science. These professionals meet the needs of the labor market, which often does not require specialization limited to a single sector, but rather a comprehensive understanding of the systems, methodologies and technologies of the entire information area, as well as the ability to understand the relationships between the various disciplines and to deal with interdisciplinary problems professionally.
The areas of learning involved in achieving the learning objectives are:
- the area of mathematical and physical disciplines to provide basic mathematical and physical knowledge necessary to correctly apply the scientific and experimental method in problem solving;
- the area of computer science disciplines to offer solid skills in computer programming and data structure management;
- the area of technologies and systems for telecommunications to provide knowledge related to signal processing and information reception and transmission;
- the area of electronic devices and circuits to provide basic skills in the design of digital and analog electronic systems and embedded systems;
- specialization courses for the acquisition of more professional skills.
COURSE STRUCTURE The Course of Studies provides a basic training, common to all engineering degrees, followed by a broad engineering training in the area of information engineering. Subsequently, the Course of Studies is structured in paths in which the student, at his own choice, will acquire specific and in-depth training in one of the areas that characterize information engineering, such as computer engineering, telecommunications engineering or electronic engineering.
In order to allow the student to acquire and integrate the various knowledge required in a harmonious and balanced way, the educational offer is structured in:
1) a basic training in which both the methodological-operational aspects of mathematics and the fundamental knowledge and methodological aspects of the basic sciences of interest for information technologies are dealt with;
2) a broad engineering training in the area of information engineering, in which the fundamental contents of the disciplines that characterize the information area (electronics, computer science and telecommunications) and the knowledge of the related methodologies are acquired.
3) At this point, students can choose between different paths:
3.1) specific and in-depth training in the field of telecommunications and the design of systems for the processing and reception-transmission of information, in order to guarantee a methodological and applicative preparation aimed at the analysis, modeling and design of systems, equipment and infrastructures concerning the acquisition, processing and transmission of information;
3.2) specific and in-depth training in the field of computer science and complex information processing systems, in order to guarantee a methodological and design preparation aimed at the design of hardware and software architectures of products and services for automation and production support;
3.3) specific and in-depth training in the field of electronics and the design of circuits and integrated systems, in order to guarantee a methodological and design preparation aimed at the analysis, modeling, design and control of hardware devices and systems of the analog and digital type concerning the methods of acquisition, processing and transmission of information and automation.
4) The training of students will be complemented by a preparation aimed at managing digital transformation and data science in computer, economic, statistical, organizational and management engineering disciplines, which the student can deepen according to his interests.
5) Students can then complete their educational path with elective courses, for example those in other areas compared to the one chosen as predominant or advanced courses.
In order to allow students to acquire an operational knowledge of the English language, the educational path necessarily includes at least three university training credits aimed at reaching an adequate linguistic level for attending teaching activities delivered in English.
The activities proposed in the last year of study also allow the student to choose whether to complete the educational path with methodological content or with project activities or internships aimed at acquiring more professional skills.
The methodological approfondissements are aimed at providing students with the necessary prerequisites to successfully continue their training in a master's degree course or in a first-level master's course and are organized in paths oriented to the disciplines of telecommunications engineering, computer science, electronics and other technical-scientific degrees such as computer science.
The course of study is strongly project-based, using the didactic tools of the laboratories and developing activities for the design of devices and software.
The professional approfondissements also provide for the acquisition of design, technological and operational skills, aimed at direct and effective insertion into the world of work, which can also be acquired through training internships at companies operating in the sector.
Knowledge required for access To enroll in the Bachelor's Degree in Computer, Communication and Electronic Engineering, it is required to have a high school diploma obtained in Italy or another qualification obtained abroad recognized as suitable.
For access to the Bachelor's Degree in Computer, Communication and Electronic Engineering, the following knowledge is also required:
Essential knowledge
- Knowledge of the Italian language
- Adequate knowledge of the English language.
Scientific knowledge
- Knowledge of the elementary notions on physical quantities and the structure of matter.
The didactic regulations describe in more detail the specific knowledge required for access to the Bachelor's Degree and specify the methods of verification of this knowledge; any additional training obligations to be completed within the first year of the course will also be specified in the event that the verification is not positive.

UNIVERSITY: University of Trento
COURSE: [INT - TPAL] TECHNIQUES OF PREVENTION IN THE ENVIRONMENT AND WORKPLACES
TYPE: Bachelor's Degree
CURRICULUM: Interuniversity Joint Degree
SECTION: General Description
URL: https://unitn.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10800

[INT - TPAL] TECHNIQUES OF PREVENTION IN THE ENVIRONMENT AND WORKPLACES Bachelor's Degree Interuniversity Joint Degree
INFO General information relating to the current academic year Duration
3 years Program
2024
Approval date
16-3-2018
Status Open Credits
180 CFU Type Bachelor's degree Category Medical, health and biological sciences Regulations Reform 270/04
Degree class L/SNT4 - Class of degrees in Health professions for prevention Interuniversity Course Participating Universities University of Trento Admission Open Admission University of Verona Department Interdepartmental Center for Medical Sciences - CISMed Verification of knowledge required for access No Program, texts and objectives Specific training objectives of the course and description of the training path Graduates in the Bachelor's Degree in Techniques of Prevention in the Environment and Workplaces must possess the theoretical and practical training and the necessary scientific basis, as required by Decree No. 58 of January 17, 1997, to exercise the profession of Prevention Technician in the environment and workplaces; the Prevention Technician in the environment and workplaces is a healthcare professional committed to promoting and protecting public health and carries out all prevention, verification and control activities in the field of environmental hygiene and safety in living and working environments, food and beverage hygiene, veterinary public health and hygiene, and environmental hygiene and protection, in accordance with the professional profile. Specifically, the graduate exercises their functions through activities of supervision, control, consultation, opinions, investigations, information, training, education, management and research. At the end of the training course, the graduate must be able to:
- Manage independently and responsibly the activities of prevention, verification and control on the quality of living and working environments
- Manage the methods of sampling and analysis of air, water, soil and food data and biological matrices for the purpose of prevention in both living and working environments;
- define the priorities of interventions based on health and safety needs, organizational needs and the optimal use of available resources;
- design, implement and deliver, in collaboration with other professionals, information and educational interventions for the control and prevention of risk factors aimed at individuals and groups;
- Use tools and methodologies for the evaluation and review of professional, organizational and relational quality;
- Manage prevention and control interventions to ensure the safety and health of food and beverages intended for consumption from the production phase to consumption
- Identify, analyze and evaluate the risks to safety and health (plants and equipment, physical, chemical, biological agents, psychosocial, organizational and ergonomic factors) and identify the most effective and appropriate prevention techniques and measures for the problem identified.
- be responsible and professional, understanding the ethical and deontological issues in relation to users and in relation to interdisciplinary and integrated collaboration with other health professionals;
- Conduct investigations and inquiries into workplace accidents and occupational diseases
- Manage verification and control interventions on the safety conditions of plants and the compliance of structures and work environments in relation to the activities connected to them
- In the activity of collective assistance in the areas of competence, identify emerging needs for safety and health, assessing where necessary the need to proceed with further specialist investigations
- Manage prevention and control interventions on public health and hygiene, interacting with other professionals on issues related to diseases transmitted by animals
- Verify the safety and health of dietary and cosmetic products
- Interpret and manage control activities in the application of legislative and regulatory provisions on health and environmental prevention within the scope and in compliance with their own skills
- Formulate opinions on public health, instruct, determine and communicate non-compliance and manage the process of adapting to safety and health criteria on the problems found
- Manage preparatory activities for the issue of authorizations or technical-health waivers for activities subject to control
- Integrate their specialist assistance in support of and in collaboration with the judicial administration in cases of investigations for environmental crimes and on the conditions of hygiene and safety in workplaces and on food that is risky for health.
- Operate in the context of health promotion and primary prevention in the area in order to promote networking, facilitate and support the management of risks in living and working environments.
- Evaluate the results of the interventions provided, adapting their planning based on the results, scientific evolution and research.
- Demonstrate teaching and tutorial skills in the field of staff training and updating their professional profile
- Manage the information systems that support the activity, interacting with other professional figures to manage and plan prevention projects within the services in which they operate.
- Contribute constructively to the development of the profession, structures and health organizations.
Training path:
1st year - aimed at providing the basic chemical-physical, biomedical and preventive hygiene knowledge, the foundations of the professional discipline as requirements for facing the first experience of internship aimed at orienting the student to the reference professional areas and acquiring basic skills.
2nd year - aimed at deepening multidisciplinary knowledge characterizing the different areas of intervention of the professional figure and professional skills relating to the methods and techniques of prevention in the different areas of competence (hygiene and health in living and working environments, food and beverages, hygiene and public health). Internship experiences are planned in the contexts in which the student can experiment with the knowledge and techniques learned.
3rd year - aimed at deepening specialization in risk and quality management processes but also at acquiring knowledge and methodologies relating to professional practice, the ability to work in teams and in complex organizational contexts. They acquire methodological skills for scientific research also to support the preparation of a thesis. The relevance assigned to internship experiences increases and the student can experience a gradual assumption of autonomy and responsibility under the supervision of experts.
This curricular logic has been concretized in the choice of credits assigned to the internship experiences which gradually increase from the 1st to the 3rd year.
Knowledge required for access Candidates in possession of a five-year secondary school diploma or another equivalent and recognized qualification obtained abroad may be admitted. The recognition of qualifications obtained abroad for the purposes of admission to the degree course is decided in compliance with the current international agreements.
The prerequisites required for the student who wishes to enroll in the course should include:
good ability in human contact, good ability in teamwork, ability to analyze and solve problems.
Admission to the Degree Course is on a programmed basis according to Law 264/99 and provides for an entrance exam. To successfully complete the Degree Course in Techniques of Prevention in the Environment and Workplaces, it is necessary to have adequate preparation in the fields of biology and chemistry.
Students admitted to the Course with a level lower than the minimum score set will be assigned additional training obligations in the disciplines of biology and chemistry with the methods specified in the Course Regulations.
